# ROUTE_MAP.md — canvas → route → backend, with implementation status

Produced by the P−1 audit (§16 step 2). Every "Live route today" cell was read
from `frontend/src/App.jsx` with its guard; every backend cell names a file that
exists under `cloudflare-worker/src/routes/`. CURRENT vs UPGRADE calls come from
reading the live page component side by side with the canvas, not from name
matching. Comparisons are source-level; the app was not run.

**Corpus:** 107 canonical `*.dc.html` canvases at `design/canvases/` (top level
only), as counted by the P−1 audit. Canvases that have landed in
`design/incoming/` since are NOT in that 107; part 5 below holds the rows for
the ones that have graduated out of the queue. `uploads/` holds 3 stale duplicates and `scraps/` holds images — both
excluded. Also outside the 107, in `canvases/shared/`: 2 standalone pitch-deck
HTMLs and 4 shared JS modules (`support.js`, `doc-page.js`, `deck-stage.js`,
`fund-model.js`) — every canvas references the runtime as a relative
`support.js` rather than inlining it — plus `assets/`. (This sentence said "at
the root" until 2026-09-07; the root of `design/canvases/` holds only its
`README.md`.)

## Status taxonomy

### Connectors Worker contract (D650, #1462)

`/api/connectors` and the temporary `/api/integrations` alias mount the same
`integrations.ts` router. `GET /available` returns the registry's category,
verified status, pulls, sends, feeds and permissions; Google Calendar and
Microsoft 365 read their existing calendar connections. `GET /` lists the
caller's connections with their enabled company IDs. `PUT /:uid/companies`
changes those links; `GET /:key` reports a teammate's connection by name only.
Credentials and personal Calendly, Carta and calendar records remain owned by
their connecting user; company links control writes into project-backed data.
D753: each `GET /available` item also carries `tagline`, `made_by`, `service`, `sign_in`,
`added`, `verified` (a recorded check, D753 decision 7), `verified_on`, `read_only`,
`page_key` and `page_href`. The answer carries `category_order` and `first_party`
(LinkedIn's card). `GET /directory/:key` answers one connector's page, descriptive
only: no connection, no `calls`. It answers 404 `unknown_provider` like `/:key`, and
needs no active company.
`GET /suggestions` reads empty module stores, `POST /requests` records a tool
request, and Super Admin `GET /requests` lists them. `GET /:uid/logs` searches
and pages activity. Every company ID supplied by a request is checked against
`user_company_links` before use. D751 (#1880): every OAuth connector with an
implementation also carries `configured` — `true` when Axal holds its client,
`false` when it does not, `null` when the key store could not be read — and no
other row carries the field.
Mercury (D710, #1775; D713, #1778; D752, #1886): an admin on HQ gets the
entity row, read from `/api/admin/mercury`, and `POST /connect` answers 409
`entity_connection_only`. A founder or partner, on either host, and an investor
on HQ get their own row, in their role's words, and connect a Read Only token.
An investor on a branch gets no row, and `POST /connect` answers 403
`purpose_not_allowed` ("Mercury is offered to your profile on HQ only.").
Everyone else, and an admin on a branch, is answered as before: `GET
/available` omits it and `POST /connect`, `GET /:key` and the waitlist and
notify-me routes answer 404 as if the key did not exist.
D752 (#1887): `GET/POST /api/mercury/personal/matches` take `target=raise|commitment` for a founder (`raise_receipts`): their own committed raise prospects and pending deal commitments. Confirming sets the prospect's `commit_status` to `wired`, or the commitment to `confirmed`.
`GET /api/revenue/projects/:projectId/mercury-credits` items carry `fits_raise_target`, and a confirmed raise receipt's line is refused `raise_receipt`.
D752 (#1889): `GET /api/revenue/projects/:projectId/mercury-spend?months=` (the project's own founder; built per request) and `POST …/mercury-spend/snapshot` `{ include_cash, confirm_visible: true }`, which upserts one `source = 'mercury'` `project_metrics` row.
`POST /api/progress/metrics/:projectId` refuses a `source` of `stripe` or `mercury` (400 `source_reserved`), and `PUT /api/progress/metrics/snapshot/:id` refuses a `mercury` row's date or figures (409 `mercury_figure_locked`).
D752 (#1888): `/api/mercury/attestations`, an investor's proof of capital (`routes/mercury_attestations.ts`, migration 491). No route returns a balance.
- `GET /` (the caller's own statements, every state, with admin views) and `POST /:uid/revoke` refuse a support session and a branch, but not a role. Another member's uid is 404 `attestation_not_found`, as a missing one is; a second revoke is 409 `already_revoked`.
- `GET /targets` (their open deal commitments, Fund I application and Fund I LP commitment, each with its cap and reason, the minimum and the consent words; no bank read), `GET /accounts` (checking and savings: id, name, kind, last four, and the holder) and `POST /` `{ purpose, target_id, account_ids, amount_cents?, consent, consent_version }` run the `capital_attestation` policy: 403 `support_session_refused`, `purpose_not_allowed` or `hq_only`.
- `POST /` refuses before any bank read with `consent_required`, `consent_outdated`, `invalid_purpose`, `invalid_target_id`, `invalid_accounts`, `target_not_found` (404, the same body for another member's target), `target_not_attestable`, `invalid_amount` or `amount_above_target`; after its two GETs with `account_not_found`, 502 `mercury_unreadable`, 502 `holder_unreadable` or 409 `balance_below_amount` (no figure). 201 stores the statement only, superseding the target's active one.
D752 (#1890): `GET /api/admin/capital-attestations` with `?purpose&target_id`, `?deal_id` or `?fund_id`; an Admin on HQ (`requireMercuryReader`); audited first (`capital_attestations_read`) or 503 `audit_unavailable`; reads `capital_attestations` and `users` only; each read counts a view on the statements whose amount it carries; a revoked statement carries no amount and no holder. `GET /api/admin/lp-applications` rows carry `proof_of_funds` as a state only.
D749 (#1838): `POST /api/admin/mercury/transactions/:uid/not-ours` `{ reason, note }` marks a posted credit Not ours (201), and `POST /api/admin/mercury/not-ours/:uid/withdraw` `{ reason }` withdraws the mark. Both are an Admin's on HQ, under the `mercury.reconcile` act.
`GET /api/admin/mercury/transactions?match=none` leaves out a line with an active mark (and `direction=credit|debit`, D717 #1856, keeps only credits or debits), `match=not_ours` lists the marked lines, and each item carries `not_ours`. The monthly cash summary and the quarterly report's `unmatched_count` leave active marks out.

### Fund administration Worker contract (D714, #1779)

`services/fundAdminAccess.ts`'s `FUND_ACTS` names every fund act, its routes and its tier. After its own `requireFundGp`, each fund write in `funds.ts` runs `assertFundAct`; the two legacy `/api/capital` issue routes and the ledger writes in `fund_ledger.ts` run `assertAdminAct`. A support session is refused every act (`403 support_session_refused`). On HQ:

- an Admin act passes `requireAdmin`, so a frozen admin gets `423 admin_frozen`;
- a Super Admin act refuses an admin without the elevation at once (`403 super_admin_required`, no step-up demand), then takes `requireSuperAdminWriteBar`, whose `step_up_required` reaches the SPA on every route.

The Super Admin acts are:

- issuing a capital call, which includes an LP's first call through `POST /api/funds/:id/lps`;
- executing a distribution;
- a `PATCH /api/funds/:id` that changes `gp_user_id`, `carried_interest` or `management_fee`;
- assigning a fund's ledger administrator.

A GP of record who is not an admin keeps today's power over their own fund, except changing its GP of record. On a branch, only the support-session refusal applies.

New routes:

- `POST /api/funds/:id/distributions/preview` returns what the distribution job would write. It uses `splitDistribution`, the same function the job runs.
- `GET /api/fund-admin/access` (`requireAdmin`, HQ only, no support session) answers `{ super_admin, acts }`.
- `GET /api/fund-admin/overview` (the same gate; PR 3b) writes #1775's `mercury_accounts_read` audit row first, or answers 503 `audit_unavailable` with no figures. It answers five blocks from the stores only, each with its own `state`: `entities` (connection and accounts as "ending NNNN"), `funds` (call totals per administered fund), `fees` (the latest fee period), `drafts` and `matches`. It never calls Mercury, and it carries no account number, routing number, token, counterparty or memo.

Every admin act leaves one `fund_admin.<act>` row in `admin_audit_log`. `COOL_OFF_ROUTES` gains the three routes that are wholly Super Admin acts. See `decisions/D714.md`.

### GitHub connector and "now fed by" (D655, #1467)

The `github` provider is a GitHub App's read-only user authorization. `GET /:uid/repositories` lists what its installations reach and what is chosen; it also answers `needs_install` (no installation on the live re-read) and `install_url` (the App’s install page from `GITHUB_APP_SLUG`, or `null`); `PUT /:uid/repositories` saves a choice and a project, after re-reading the grant and refusing any permission beyond the registry's four read scopes (`write_scope_refused`). Both need the active company enabled for the connection, and the project must be in it. `POST /:uid/sync` lands issues on `roadmap_okrs` and pull requests on `mvp_features`, linked through `connector_fed_items` (migration 445) so a re-sync updates; releases are recorded with no store. `GET /:uid/sync-status` returns the running sync's percentage and what it is reading. `GET /:uid/fed` returns, for every connector, a count per module it feeds; a connector that does not record deliveries says Not recorded, never 0.

`DELETE /:uid` on a GitHub connection also revokes the grant on GitHub, best effort. Its user token is renewed 60 s before it expires, and a refused renewal sets the connection to `error` with `github_sign_in_expired`.

D751 (#1880), for all nine OAuth connectors (Calendly, Carta, DocuSign, GitHub, HubSpot, Salesforce, Slack, Stripe, Zoom): `GET /oauth/:provider/start` checks readiness before it writes any state. An unset client is 503 `oauth_unconfigured` ("<Name> isn't set up on Axal yet…"), and an admin's body adds `env_vars` and, except for Zoom, `admin_href: '/admin?tab=integration-keys'`; an unreadable key store is 503 `oauth_setup_unreadable` with `Retry-After: 60`; a `coming_soon` connector is 503 `coming_soon`, the same body as `POST /connect`. None of them writes an `oauth_state_tokens` row. A client that goes missing mid-flow reaches the page as `reason=not_configured`. See `decisions/D751.md`.

### Fund notices and booklets Worker contract (D716, #1781)

The Subscription Booklet's store is mounted at `/api/fund-booklets` (`routes/fund_booklets.ts`, `services/subscriptionBooklet.ts`, migration 478).
- **The applicant** (an approved Fund I `lp_applications` row; anyone else gets 404):
  - `POST /mine` opens the booklet, or returns the live one.
  - `GET /mine` answers the parts, the fields with their ticks and `group` (the nearest counsel heading above each, for the screen only, never in the digest; #1866), `missing_parts` and the digest.
  - `PUT /mine/answers { answers: [{ field_id, checked }] }`: 400 `field_unknown`, 409 `booklet_not_open`.
  - `POST /mine/submit { digest }`: 409 `booklet_part_missing` (naming the parts) or `content_changed`.
  - `GET /mine/paper` answers `{ html, digest, page_size }`: the booklet on #1776's paper, screen HTML with the faces inline, `no-store`. `?part=2..9` draws that one part alone, with no cover and no contents, and the whole booklet's digest; any other value is 400 `part_invalid` (#1866). A submitted booklet prints its frozen text (D759).
  - `GET /mine` and the Admin's `GET /:uid` carry `edition` (v[N]) and `signing: { state, signed_at, accepted_at }` (`not_sent`, `sent`, `awaiting_gp`, `accepted`, `void`); the Admin's read also `gp_sent` (D759).
  - `POST /mine/sign` (D759) sends the submitted booklet for the applicant's signature (Parts 3 to 8) and answers 201 `{ uid, stage: 'applicant', envelope_uuid, token }`, `no-store`; the token goes to the caller, its recipient, alone. Refusals in order: 404 `booklet_not_found`, 403 `support_session_refused`, 403 `hq_only`, 409 `booklet_not_submitted`, 409 `booklet_already_sent` (`state`), 409 `booklet_part_missing` (`parts`), 409 `booklet_text_changed`, 503 `booklet_send_failed`.
  - A support session cannot write.
- **An Admin on HQ** (`hq_only` on a branch; support session refused; D714 acts):
  - `GET /` lists the booklets, with no ticks (`fund_booklet.read`).
  - `GET /:uid` answers one booklet with its ticks, audited (`fund_booklet.read`).
  - `GET /:uid/pdf` draws its PDF on request through Browser Run (never pdf-lib), keeps nothing, and is audited (`fund_booklet.read`); 503 `booklet_render_unavailable` when the print fails.
  - `POST /:uid/withdraw { reason }` takes a reason of 10–200 characters (`fund_booklet.withdraw`); it voids the booklet's pending envelopes, and an accepted booklet is 409 `booklet_accepted` (D759).
  - `POST /:uid/acceptance` (D759, `fund_booklet.accept`, Super Admin) sends the General Partner's stage again and answers 201 `{ uid, stage: 'gp', envelope_uuid }`, no token; 404 `booklet_not_found`, 409 `booklet_not_signed`, `booklet_already_sent`, `fund_signer_missing` or `booklet_text_changed`.
- **The e-sign signer routes, for a booklet envelope** (`document_type = 'subscription_booklet'`, D759): `GET /api/legal/esign/sign/:token` adds `booklet: { stage, edition }`; `GET …/paper` prints the frozen parts with the other stage's lines as signed; `POST …` draws the signed copy with this signer's image on their own lines, refuses 409 `booklet_text_changed` before the claim, and on the applicant's completion sends the General Partner's stage. Every other envelope answers as before.

Fund notices are mounted at `/api/fund-notices` (`routes/fund_notices.ts`, `services/fundNotices.ts`, `services/fundNoticeStore.ts`, migration 478). See `decisions/D716.md`.
- **An Admin on HQ** (`hq_only` on a branch; support session refused):
  - `GET /types`.
  - `POST /preview { type, source, inputs, page_size }` answers `{ html, paper_sha256, figures, figures_sha256, refusal }`, with fonts omitted, Draft and `no-store` (`fund_notice.preview`).
  - `POST /calls/:callUid/preview` gives each line's figures and one digest (`fund_notice.preview`).
  - `GET /` and `GET /:uid` read the notices; `GET /:uid/pdf` serves the kept PDF.
- **The Super Admin** (`fund_notice.issue`, `super_admin_required` before any TOTP or step-up):
  - `POST /issue { type, source, inputs, page_size, figures_sha256, reason? }`;
  - `POST /calls/:callUid/issue { page_size, digest }`.
- **The LP** (only notices addressed to their own LP row; anyone else gets 404):
  - `GET /mine` (with `superseded`, `instructions_changed`, and `call_number` and `due_date` computed on read, #1860);
  - `GET /mine/:uid` (the same, and the frozen figures);
  - `GET /mine/:uid/pdf`.
- **A PDF** is drawn once per stamp through Browser Run (never pdf-lib), kept in R2, and answered `private, no-store`.

### Money rails Worker contract (D707, #1772)

`GET /api/admin/money-rails` (`admin_money_rails.ts`, admin only) answers `{ entities, kinds, stripe }`:
- `entities`: each Axal entity's key, name and role (`services/moneyRails.ts`'s `ENTITY_ROLES`);
- `kinds`: the eleven `MONEY_KINDS` rows, each with its payer, payee, rail and group;
- `stripe`: `{ account_holder: 'management', mode }`, where `mode` is `stripeMode`'s `test`, `live` or `unconfigured`.

It answers no key and no account number. `stripeCall` refuses a Stripe create with no money kind, or with a fund or intercompany kind, before any request leaves the Worker. `POST /api/payments/intent` refuses a body with no `price_id` (`money_kind_required`). Neither has a frontend method.

### Legal paper Worker contract (D711, #1776)

Every legal document draws one HTML paper (`services/legalPaper.ts`), printed to PDF by Browser Run through `BROWSER.quickAction('pdf', …)`, with pdf-lib (`services/pdf.ts`) as the fallback. Every kept or handed-out file gets an append-only `legal_document_files` row (migration 475). See `decisions/D711.md`. Since #1865 the paper's tokens are R2's, and a stub's paper (`preview.pdf`, `render`, `render.pdf` and the thumbnail) reads "No body in the store yet." and, for a counsel slug, "Stub — counsel to draft."

New routes:
- `POST /api/admin/contracts/templates/render` · admin: `{ title, body, slug, category, version, page_size, cover, resolve, fonts }` → `{ html, body_sha256, page_size, paper_sha256, state }`, `no-store`. A body over 256 KB is 413 `body_too_large`.
- `GET /api/admin/contracts/templates/paper-fonts` · admin: `{ css, paper_sha256 }`, `private, max-age=86400`.
- `GET /api/admin/contracts/templates/store/:slug/export.docx?resolve=&page=` · admin: a Word file, after its `docx_export` row is written; 503 `export_unrecorded` and no file when the row cannot be written. Binary, outside the typed drift surface.
- `GET /api/admin/contracts/:uid/files` · the envelope's creator or a Super Admin: per file, its kind, renderer, page size, hash, size, `created_at`, whether a stored copy exists, and `signer`. Never a storage key, name or email.
- `GET /api/legal/esign/sign/:token/paper` · the signing token: `{ html, body_sha256, page_size }`, `no-store`; the token, expiry and void checks of `GET /sign/:token`, with no audit event.

Changed routes:
- `POST /api/legal/esign/sign/:token` writes `esign/signed/<uuid>/<file uid>.pdf` and its `signed_copy` row in the batch that signs.
- `GET /api/legal/esign/sign/:token/document` serves the recipient's own newest copy (else `signed_r2_key`).
- `POST /api/admin/contracts/envelopes/preview` and `/send`, and `POST /api/legal/esign/send`, take `page_size` (`letter`|`a4`) and `cover` (`dark`|`light`), else 400 `page_size_invalid` / `cover_invalid`. The preview answers `renderer` and `page_size`.
- `GET /api/admin/contracts/templates/store/:slug/preview.pdf` takes `?page=` and `?cover=` and sends `X-Axal-Renderer`.
- The DocuSign send writes a `docusign_source` row, and each reconcile a `docusign_signed` row (one per file).

### Fund fees Worker contract (D709, #1774)

`/api/fund-fees/:fundId` (`fund_fees.ts`). Every route opens with `requireFundGp` (the GP of record or an admin; 401, 402 or 404 otherwise). Every write then asserts its D714 act (`assertFundAct`): confirm (`fee_terms.confirm`) and issue (`fee_period.issue`) are Super Admin acts, `super_admin_required` (403) to a plain Admin first, then `requireSuperAdminWriteBar`; every other write is `fee.write`. A support session gets `support_session_refused` (403) on every write.
- `GET /`: terms versions and their defaults, billable LPs with admission dates (a missing one flagged), net invested records, offsets, periods.
- `POST /terms` writes a `draft` version; `POST /terms/:termsUid/confirm` makes it `confirmed`, the only state that bills.
- `PUT /lps/:lpId/admission`, `POST /net-invested`, `POST /offsets`.
- `POST /periods/:quarter/preview` saves the quarter's draft and answers its `digest`; a refusal saves nothing.
- `POST /periods/:quarter/issue` with `{ digest, funding_call_uid? }`: a digest that no longer matches is 409; issuing an issued quarter with its digest answers `already_issued: true` and writes nothing.
- `POST /periods/:quarter/funding-call` with `{ call_uid }` links issued lines to their LPs' lines in that call.
- `GET /periods/:quarter` (period, lines, `collected`: the posted date and last four of a confirmed Mercury match, else null, D710; and `remittance`) and `GET /periods/:quarter/statements/:lpId` (its `pay_to` is never due, plus the same `remittance`). On Fund I the remittance is #1773's `payInstructionsFor('gp', 'management_fee')`, masked with its warning, or its refusal; on any other fund it is `not_an_axal_fund` and the registry is not read.

`:quarter` is `YYYYQn`. An LP, terms version or call of another fund is 404. Issuing records amounts and the `AXAL-MF-<quarter>-L<lp>` and `-F<fund>` references; nothing moves money, and no route has a frontend method yet (#1779's `fundFeesApi`).

### AI allowance Worker contract (D640, #1433)

The Usage page work in #1430 uses the mounted `ai_budget.ts` routes. `GET /api/ai-budget/me` returns the caller's allowance and paid balance with `held_for_review` (UID, time, held micro-USD and recorded cost); a review hold blocks new runs without hiding that read. Super Admin routes `GET /api/ai-budget/reviews` and `POST /api/ai-budget/reviews/:uid/charge|refund` list and settle those holds with a recorded reason and actor. `GET /api/ai-budget/caps` lists all ten plan keys, their effective daily/monthly allowances and whether each value is recorded or from the environment; `PUT /api/ai-budget/caps/:licence` remains the audited write. Since D750 (#1841), `GET /api/ai-budget/top-up` answers `{configured:false}`, `{configured:true,state:'ready',product}` or `{configured:true,state:'not_ready'}` (503 only when D1 or Stripe cannot be read), and the Super Admin route `GET /api/ai-budget/top-up/status` returns `{state, reason, price_id_suffix, mode}` for the AI allowances screen. These are Worker routes for #1435's screens (moved from S09), not frontend API methods added by this issue.


### Eadwyn AI rail Worker contract (D720)

`routes/ai.ts`, mounted at `/api/ai`, and one route of `routes/ai_budget.ts`, mounted at `/api/ai-budget`. Every route is signed in (`requireAuth`). Except for the HQ catalogue explicitly gated below, these routes read and write the caller's own rows only and take no user id from the body or the query. The owner's runbook is `documentation/operations/AIRAIL.md`.

A page is a page key, `<Profile> · <Section>[ · <Module>]` (D720 §5): the browser's page map (`frontend/src/lib/railPages.js`, D768) gives a route its key, and the matrix (`cloudflare-worker/src/services/ai/railMatrix.json`, D764) gives a key its models. A key can hold a `/`, so it travels in the query or the body, never in the path. A run carries no route of its own: every request names the page in `X-Rail-Page`, the open option in `X-Rail-Option` and the record in `X-Rail-Record` (`util/railContext.ts`, D765 and D770), and `run()` records the run under them and checks the page's switch, answering 403 `assist_off` in the switch's words when it is off.

Prices are selected by `billingMode` in `services/ai/pricing.ts`, which currently returns `cost`. D762 (#2019) will make it read `AI_BILLING` (`wrangler.toml`, both blocks): `"charged"` will bill Cloudflare's cost × `AI_MARKUP` (1.25), and anything else cost. The admin licence always pays cost. Every route below that quotes a price gives `cost` and `billed` for the caller's licence, and `billing` says which one a run is settled at.

| Route | Who | What |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GET /api/ai/me/spend` | signed in | The caller's month-to-date and today figures, the caps the router enforces, and their last run. `?surface=<path>` narrows it to one page's month and last run (D552); a value that is not an app path exactly as the router records one is refused with 400. |
| `GET /api/ai/pricing` | signed in | The router's own tables: `unit` (`usd_per_1m_tokens`), `prices` (`PRICE_USD_PER_1M_TOKENS`), `audio_prices` (per audio minute), and `routes`, each task's `model`, `fallback_chain` and `alternates`. The rail draws "Default" only on a task's `model` (D400). |
| `GET /api/ai/rates` | signed in | The Usage page's rate card (D641): `billing`, and one row per model a price table holds, with its name, its unit prices at `cost` and `billed`, `retired: true` on a model kept only so past runs keep their cost, and `image: true` on a model priced by size and steps (D766). Read from the tables on every request. |
| `GET /api/ai/catalogue` | signed in | The models Workers AI offers today (D760): status `ga` or `partner`, with a price, past the partner gate. Each with its `kind`, `status`, `paid_only`, `partner`, `context_window` and unit prices. Ordered by kind, the safety model last. Also `billing`, and `guard.every_run`. |
| `GET /api/ai/rail?page=<key>[&option=<key>]` | signed in | One page's matrix row (D764): its `crumbs`, `kind`, `models` (each with `name`, `role`, `line` and unit prices), its `options` with their own models and picks, the member's `pick` and `mode` for the page, `billing`, and `guard` (the safety model, `every_run: true`). A key the matrix has no row for is 404 `unknown_page`; an option key that is not `<verb>:<object>` is 400 `invalid_option`. A stored pick the row no longer offers is deleted and reads as none. |
| `PUT /api/ai/rail/pick` | signed in | `{ page, option?, model }`: the member's model for the page, or for one of its options. A model the row does not offer is 400 `model_not_offered`, with the row's `models`; `null` clears the pick. |
| `PUT /api/ai/rail/mode` | signed in | `{ page, mode: 'eadwyn' \| 'manual' \| null }`: the page's switch (D769). Anything else is 400 `invalid_mode`; `null` clears it. |
| `GET /api/ai/rail-preferences` | signed in | The caller's rail `modes` and `models`, by section (D698, migration 465's `ai_rail_preferences`). |
| `PUT /api/ai/rail-preferences/:section` | signed in | `{ mode: 'eadwyn' \| 'manual' \| null }`; anything else is 400 `invalid_mode`. `null` clears it. At most `RAIL_PREFERENCE_CAP` (1,000) rows per member, shared with `/rail/pick` and `/rail/mode`. |
| `PUT /api/ai/rail-preferences/:section/:task` | signed in | `{ model: string \| null }`: one of the task's `alternates`, else 400 `model_not_offered`; a task with no choice is refused. `null` clears the pick. |
| `POST /api/ai/workspace/explain` | signed in | `{ workspace, zone?, coverage: string[], model?, page? }`: reads back one workspace zone from the coverage lines the rail already draws, never the rows behind them. `workspace` is required (400). The run goes through `run()`, which owns the model allow-list, the budget and Llama Guard (D763), and records `page` as its surface. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/runs?group=page\|option\|record&month=YYYY-MM` | signed in | Where the member's own runs went this month, or the month named (D776): one row per page, per option within a page, or per record, at most `MAX_CONSUMPTION_ROWS`, with `runs`, `charged_microusd` (null when no run recorded a charge), the `allowance_microusd` and `tokens_microusd` it came from, and `not_recorded`; then `truncated` and the month's `totals`. A safety check's run is counted in what it cost, never as a run. Another `group` is 400 `invalid_group`, a malformed month 400 `invalid_month`. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/cost?range=mtd\|30d\|prev_month&group=model\|page\|option` | signed in | The member's own cost (D787): `range` (`from` and `to`, UTC days, inclusive), `totals` (`runs`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `charged_microusd`, `refused`, `not_recorded`; D805 (#2243) adds `by_kind`, `{ tokens, audio_speech, image, other }` each with `cost_usd` and `charged_microusd` by the model's catalogue kind (`KIND_GROUP`), `other_models`, every model counted in `other` including one the catalogue does not know, and `output_not_recorded`, runs on an output-token-priced model that recorded no output count), `days` (every day in the range, each with its `by_model`), `models` (each with its catalogue `name`, its tokens, its non-token `units`, `cost_usd` and `charged_microusd`), `groups` by `group`, and `truncated`. A refused run counts in `refused` only; a guard run counts under the guard model; a charge not recorded is `null`, never 0. At most `MAX_COST_RUNS` (10,000) runs, newest first. `range` defaults to `mtd`, `group` to `model`; anything else is 400 `invalid_range` or `invalid_group`. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/cost.csv` | signed in | The same query and read as CSV, one row per day and model with a run: `day,model,name,kind,runs,input_tokens,output_tokens,cost_usd,charged_microusd` (`kind` from D805). |
| `GET /api/ai/catalogue/hq` | HQ Admin or Super Admin | D776 PR 2 (#2093), the one route here that is not the caller's own: the billing mode (`billingMode`), `AI_MARKUP`, and each offered model priced at cost and billed per unit, with `paid_only` and the D764 matrix pages that name it. |

### AI credits and token packs Worker contract (D755, #1870)

D755 sells the AI top-up balance as token packs (`ai_token_packs`, migration 486) and records every movement of it in the append-only `ai_credit_ledger`. The routes below are Worker routes; the screens that draw them are CR4–CR8.

| Route | Who | What it answers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/top-up` | signed in | `state` (`unset`, `not_ready`, `ready`), `packs` on sale, `on_hold`, and `latest` (the last purchase in 24 hours: `waiting`, `overdue`, `credited`, `refunded` or `disputed`). D750's `configured` and `product` stay, deprecated. Never a reason. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/history?before=&limit=&runs=1` | signed in | The member's own ledger, newest first, paged on `(created_at, uid)`, with `pending` purchases on the first page. `runs=1` adds hold, release and spend. Never an adjustment's reason. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/limit`, `PUT /api/ai-budget/limit` | signed in; `PUT` needs a recent sign-in (TOTP, stepped up) | D788: the member's own monthly AI spend limit. `GET` answers `limit_microusd` (null for none), this UTC month's `charged_microusd`, `share_used`, `resets_on`, `notify` and the plan's `allowance`. `PUT {monthly_limit_usd: number\|null, notify: {50, 80, 100}}`: `null` removes it; a negative amount, more than two decimals or more than $100,000 is 400 `invalid_spend_limit` with `field`. A run past the limit is refused at its hold: 402 `spend_limit`. D805 (#2243): `GET` also answers `pack_orders` (`today`, `per_day`, `window_hours`, `resets_at`): the member's token-pack orders in the rolling 24 hours, auto-reloads included, counted by `recentPackOrders`, the function the purchase check refuses with. D792 part 2 (#2270): that count is read on its own; when it cannot be read, `GET` still answers 200 with the limit and allowance, `pack_orders: null` and `pack_orders_reason: 'pack_orders_unreadable'`. A limit that cannot be read is 503 `ai_spend_limit_unreadable`. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/top-up/status` | Super Admin | D750's single line, plus `packs`: one line per pack with its state, reason, the last six characters of its price ID, and `adoptable` for the `STRIPE_PRICE_AI_TOPUP` fallback. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/members/:userId` | Super Admin | One member's allowance, balance, held top-up, ledger (with reasons), purchases and shortfalls. Each read is logged. |
| `POST /api/ai-budget/members/:userId/adjustments` | Super Admin | `{amount_usd, reason, idempotency_key}`: at most $100 either way, at most $250 added in 30 days, never below zero, a reason of 10–500 characters. In the D248 cool-off list. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/reconcile` | Super Admin | Balances that differ from their ledger, purchases paid over an hour ago and not credited, and open disputes. It credits nothing. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/auto-reload` | signed in, TOTP with a recent step-up | D794 (#2219): the member's auto-reload settings, and the saved card's brand and last four (only a card on their own Stripe customer). |
| `PUT /api/ai-budget/auto-reload` | signed in, TOTP with a recent step-up; 423 during a recovery cool-off | `{ enabled, pack_uid, threshold_usd, setup_intent_id? }`. A pack HQ sells and has not archived, on sale to turn it on; a threshold from $0 to $500 with two decimals at most; a card only from this member's completed auto-reload SetupIntent, read back from Stripe. Logged without the card. |
| `POST /api/ai-budget/auto-reload/setup` | as above | An off-session SetupIntent on the member's own Stripe customer for auto-reload; only its client secret is returned. |
| `GET /api/ai-budget/members/:userId/runs?month=` | HQ Admin or Super Admin | D776 PR 2 (#2093): one member's month by option within a page, with totals, and each run (newest first, at most 100) with its model, page, option, cost and charge. Each read is logged (`ai_member_runs_read`, the member's id and the month). |
| `POST /api/admin/catalog/token-packs` | Super Admin | `{amount_usd, idempotency_key}`: the Worker creates the product and its one-time USD price in Stripe with Idempotency-Keys, then the pack row, off sale. |
| `POST /api/admin/catalog/token-packs/adopt` | Super Admin | `{price_id}`: records an existing one-time USD price of whole dollars from $5 to $500. |
| `PATCH /api/admin/catalog/token-packs/:uid` | Super Admin | `on_sale`, `bonus_usd` (0–10% of face) and `sort`. The amount never changes. |
| `POST /api/admin/catalog/token-packs/:uid/archive` | Super Admin | Off sale for good, and the Stripe price archived. |
| `GET /api/public/plans-and-packs` | anyone | Every plan with its price and AI allowance, the packs on sale, `covers` and `resets`. D1 only, cached 60 seconds, its own rate-limit bucket, no person named. |

D806 (#2245): Billing's invoices and the card's address (`routes/billing.ts`, `services/billingInvoices.ts`).

| Route | Who | What it answers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GET /api/billing/invoices?page=1&per=25` | signed in | The member's own invoices in one list, newest first, then paged (`per` 1–100, default 25), with `page`, `per` and `total`. Each row is `{ kind: 'order'\|'stripe', id, date, description, amount_cents, currency, status, pdf_url }`; `status` is `paid`, `refunded`, `partly_refunded`, `open` or `void`. Orders are the paid ones in D1: "AI tokens, $25 pack", "AI tokens, auto-reload $25" (a line marked `origin: 'auto_reload'`), else the items' names; refunds are read for token packs only, from the ledger's refund rows; the PDF is `/api/orders/:ref/invoice`. Plan invoices are each Stripe customer the member has (founder, investor, read once if the same), at most 100 per customer: "[plan] · monthly", their credit notes, and Stripe's `invoice_pdf` passed through as an https URL. When Stripe cannot be read the orders still list, with `degraded: ['stripe']`. |
| `GET /api/billing/overview?scope=` | signed in | Unchanged but for D806: each of `payment_methods` carries `billing_address` (`line1`, `line2`, `city`, `postal_code`, `country`) from Stripe's `billing_details.address`, or `null` when Stripe holds none. |

A pack is bought only through `POST /api/orders/intent`, alone, at face value, with no promo code. `POST /api/payments/intent`, `/api/payments/alacarte/intent`, `/api/payments/promo/validate` and `/api/billing/subscription/swap/preview|confirm` answer 400 `token_pack_checkout_only` for a pack price, and the generic `/api/admin/catalog` writes answer 409 `token_pack_managed_on_hq`. `/api/admin/catalog` is in the cool-off prefixes.

The Stripe webhook (`POST /api/billing/stripe/webhook`) credits a pack only on `payment_intent.succeeded`, once, with its bonus. Three new events take tokens back (`services/aiCreditReversals.ts`): `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created` and `charge.dispute.closed`. Each acts only on a token pack's charge.

Introduction packs retire with CR5 (#1915): `POST /api/payments/intro-credits/intent` answers 410 `intro_packs_retired`, and `GET /api/introductions/packs` answers `{packs: [], on_sale: false}`. Credits already bought still spend.

**`/plans-and-pricing` (CR5, #1915).** Every plan card shows its AI allowance from `GET /api/public/plans-and-packs` (PR 2, #2045), and the Token packs section (`#token-packs`) is CR7's (#1985). Since PR 3 the page opens without sign-in (D755 §13; `isPublicPath` lists it):
- a visitor sees the plan cards, built from the public read because `/api/catalog/products` needs a session, and the Token packs with Sign in to buy (`/login?next=%2Fplans-and-pricing%23token-packs`);
- Subscribe and Preview go to `/login?next=%2Fplans-and-pricing`;
- the promo box, the cart, the add-ons, active licences, the product modal, the admin tools and the phone's account bar are not drawn for a visitor, and their reads are not made;
- `/plans-and-pricing/:productId`, `/checkout` and `/checkout/confirmation` still need sign-in;
- `/products` redirects with its query and hash, so `/products#token-packs` lands on the packs.

The public `/pricing` page takes its paid tiers' prices from the same read; a tier the read does not price says "—" and carries no Schema.org price. The door pages' tokens block links to `/plans-and-pricing#token-packs`.

### Bank accounts Worker contract (D708, #1773)

`/api/admin/bank-accounts` (`admin_bank_accounts.ts`) is the Super Admin's registry of each Axal entity's bank accounts, on HQ only; a branch refuses. `GET /` needs `requireSuperAdmin` and returns every account masked (`••••NNNN`), `coverage` (the resolver's answer per payment purpose), `fund_i` (the fund carrying the `spinout-fund-i` slug, or null) and `refusals` (D717, #1856: `{ code, message }` for the four refusals the console explains, each in the resolver's own sentence). Each write and the reveal first refuse an admin without the elevation with 403 `super_admin_required`, then take `requireSuperAdminWriteBar`: `POST /` adds (201), `PATCH /:uid` takes `label` and `purposes` only (anything else is 409 `instructions_immutable`), `POST /:uid/retire` takes a reason, and `POST /:uid/reveal` takes a reason, writes `entity_bank_account_reveals` first and answers one number with `Cache-Control: no-store`. Migration 472 holds the number only as `v1.` ciphertext, and its triggers refuse any change in place.

The resolver is `services/payInstructions.ts`. `payInstructionsFor` gives masked instructions and the warning; `revealPayInstructions` records a reveal and then decrypts. Its refusals are `payee_not_entitled`, `fund_account_missing`, `no_active_account`, `holder_not_payee`, `savings_account`, `ambiguous_account` and `account_unreadable`. A capital call on Fund I is refused with that code (409, nothing written) on `POST /api/funds/:id/capital-call`, `POST /api/funds/:id/lps` with `first_call_cents`, both legacy `/api/capital` call routes, and the `capital_call_notice` job, which writes `capital_call_held` instead. `POST /api/funds/:id/capital-calls/preview` adds `payment_instructions`. Moving the slug onto or off a fund through `PATCH /api/funds/:id` is the Super Admin's. `GET /api/funds/lp-portal` adds `pay_instructions` to each unpaid Fund I line (masked, with the reference `AXAL-CC-<fund>-L<lp>-<line>`). `GET /api/funds/:id/capital-calls/lines/:lineId/pay-instructions` gives the LP whose line it is the full instructions, recorded first; a support session gets 403, anyone else 404, and a paid line 409. Writes, the reveals and the LP's GET share the fail-closed `bank_accounts` rate-limit bucket (10 a minute per user).

| Canvas | Live route | Component and Worker | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Legal Documents · Templates.dc.html` · L11 ("The bank-account registry · super admin"; D717, #1856; D708 decision 14 had drawn none) | `/admin?tab=bank-accounts` · Super Admin; on the HQ · Platform strip after Payments | `pages/admin/consoles/BankAccountsConsole.jsx`; `admin_bank_accounts.ts`, `GET /api/admin/mercury/connections`, `GET /api/admin/mercury/transactions?match=none&direction=credit` | CURRENT |
| `Legal Documents · Templates.dc.html` · L12 `p-call` ("Capital-call notice") | `/spinout-lab/investor-workspace` My commitment · an LP; Wire details on an unpaid Fund I line | `pages/SpinoutLabLpCommitment.jsx` (`WireDetails`, `:39`); `GET /api/funds/lp-portal`, `GET /api/funds/:id/capital-calls/lines/:lineId/pay-instructions` | UPGRADE: #1859 opens it on the amount and due date, with the instructions behind the phone check |

### Territory licence Administrator's account Worker contract (D798, #2231)

These are Worker routes in `routes/licence.ts`, backed by `services/licenceAccount.ts`. Each one resolves the caller's own licence through `licence_admins`, as a principal or a delegate. None takes a licence from the request. A caller who administers no licence gets 404 `no_licence`, as `GET /api/licence/mine` does. On a branch, a non-admin is refused with 403 first. An admin then reads `{ available: false, reason: "Read on HQ; this branch has no copy yet" }` and no figure, because no RPC reads these yet. The statements read adds `usage.send_now: true`, and `needs` lists the branch's own notices and seats. The frontend methods are `licenceAccountApi` in `frontend/src/lib/api/licenceAccount.js`. This task adds no UI.

| Route | Who | What it answers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GET /api/licence/mine/contract` | the licence's Administrator (principal or delegate) | `in_force` is the latest signed contract. `pending` is the latest one sent and not superseded. `earlier` lists every other contract HQ has sent, with `superseded_at`. Each has uid, template title and version, status (following the envelope), `sent_at`, `signed_at`, `unfilled_fields` and the stored `body_md`, never re-rendered. A contract HQ has not sent is never shown. `sign_url` is the caller's own `/esign/<token>` link for `pending`, given only while it is sent to them and the link has not expired. Otherwise it is null, with `sign_reason`. |
| `GET /api/licence/mine/contract/:uid/pdf` | the licence's Administrator | One visible contract printed from its stored body (`paperModel` with `preserveBody`, then `renderLegalPdf`). A draft, another licence's contract or an unknown uid answers 404; a failed render answers 503; a branch answers 501 `contract_hq_only`. |
| `GET /api/licence/mine/statements` | the licence's Administrator | `statements` lists the `issued`, `paid` and `void` statements; a draft is never sent. Each carries period, gross, share bps, owed, paid, disputed, status, currency, `streams` (as stored in `streams_json`) and `payment_reference` (`AXAL-LIC-<licence_ref>-<period>`). `drawn_by_user_id`, `paid_by_user_id` and HQ's notes are never sent. `usage_reports` gives this licence's reports by period. `usage` gives each period's status: `sent` with its date, `late` or `due` against D797's report day, or `not_sent` with `late: null` until that day is recorded. On HQ, `usage.send_now` is false with `send_now_reason`. `payment` is the `territory_licence_fee` account, masked as `paymentBlock` masks it (last four digits only), with `method`, `payee_entity`, `payee_name` and the warning. When no account carries the purpose, or the licence's IP licensor is not the payee, it is a refusal sentence instead. |
| `GET /api/licence/mine/needs` | the licence's Administrator | The Summary's "Needs you" list and its count, in order: a contract waiting for the caller's own signature; HQ's notices waiting for the caller's answer; issued, unpaid statements; and, on a branch only, seat types at 90% or more of their seats licensed. Each item has `kind`, `title`, `link`, `ref` and `due`. `reasons` says what could not be listed, and why. |
| `POST /api/licence/mine/usage/send` | the licence's Administrator, on a branch | "Send now": the branch's own usage push (D266), run on demand. It answers 200 with the result, or 502 with the reason when the push could not be sent. D808: at most once per five minutes per branch through `RATE_LIMITS`, so a press inside the window answers 429 `send_now_refused` with `retry_after`. A missing or failing throttle store answers 503 `send_now_refused`. HQ is called in neither case. HQ answers 501 `usage_branch_only`. |
| `POST /api/licence/mine/statements/:uid/dispute` | the licence's Administrator | Takes `{ cents, reason }`: `cents` is a positive whole number, no more than owed, and `reason` is 10 to 2,000 characters; anything else is 400 `invalid_dispute` with the `field`. It answers 201 with `escalation`: an `hq_escalations` row with `branch_code` `hq`, kind `other`, subject "Statement dispute <period>", `subject_ref` set to the licence uid, and due in 72 hours. Every Super Admin is notified. It writes no statement field. Another licence's statement or a draft answers 404, and a void one answers 409 `not_disputable`. On a branch it answers 501 `statement_hq_only`. |

### Licence requests Worker contract (D799, #2232)

An Administrator requests and HQ decides (D795, D-d). The requests are escalations (migration 259): four new kinds, `renewal` (72 h), `terms_change` (120 h), `delegate` (72 h) and `non_renewal` (72 h), beside `moderation`, `content`, `seat_increase` and `other`. Each request kind's fields are `services/escalationRequests.ts`'s, one rule and one refusal sentence per kind, and they travel in `detail` as `{ v: 1, request, note }`, so no migration was needed. HQ's answer writes one `notifications_inbox` row for the raiser (`services/escalationAnswerNotice.ts`): `in_app` and `email`, category `compliance`, type `licence_request_answered`, link `/admin/my-licence/requests`. There is no UI yet: the page is D800's and HQ's answer screen D801's. The SPA's client is `lib/api/licenceRequests.js`.

| Method and path | Who | Worker | What |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GET /api/licence/mine/requests` | an Administrator (`licence_admins`); anyone else `no_licence` 404 | `routes/licence_requests.ts` → `listLicenceRequests` | On HQ: the caller's own licence's requests (`branch_code = 'hq'`, `subject_ref` = the licence uid), each with `request`, `note` and `sla`, plus the kinds it offers and hides, each kind's response hours, the requestable terms and the seat types. On a branch: the branch lane's `GET /api/branch/escalations`. |
| `POST /api/licence/mine/requests` | the same | `routes/licence_requests.ts` → `recordLicenceRequest` | On HQ: writes one row, open while the licence is suspended; refuses a hidden kind (D206), a request kind without its fields, an unknown field, a client notice date, and a 21st open request (409 `too_many_open`), each with its sentence and no row. On a branch: the branch lane's `POST /api/branch/escalations`. |
| `POST /api/branch/escalations` | a branch admin | `routes/branch_escalations.ts` | Now also takes `request`: required for the four new kinds, checked when sent for `seat_increase`, refused on any other kind; stored in `detail` and sent to HQ, which checks it again (`recordEscalation`). |
| `GET /api/admin/escalations` | Super Admin | `routes/admin_escalations.ts` | Each row also carries `request` and `note`. |
| `PATCH /api/admin/escalations/:uid`, `POST …/:uid/resend` | Super Admin | `routes/admin_escalations.ts` | On an `hq` row the decision is the raiser's bell notice, reported as `notified`, not pushed. A branch's raiser is told by the branch when the decision lands (`applyEscalationAnswer`). |

### Product testing Worker contract (D812, #2258)

These are Worker routes in `routes/testing.ts`, backed by `services/testing.ts` and migration 516. Three routers share one set of rules. Every change writes one `testing_events` row, in the same batch and only when the change happened. The bell is told in-app. A partner's programme is draft, then submitted, approved by HQ, opened by HQ, and closed. HQ's own programme opens from draft. Picks never exceed `places`: the limit is checked in the same `UPDATE`. The frontend methods are `productTestingApi`, `partnerTestingApi` and `adminTestingApi` in `frontend/src/lib/api/productTesting.js`. This task adds no UI (D814, D815).

| Route | Who | What it answers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GET /api/testing/programmes` | signed in | `items`: open programmes, between `opens_at` and `closes_at`, whose `eligible_roles` hold the caller's role and with a place left. Each has `places_left` and `my_status`. Also `consent`: the sentence a member agrees to and its version. |
| `GET /api/testing/applications/mine` | signed in | `items`: the caller's applications, with the programme's name, dates and status. |
| `POST /api/testing/programmes/:uid/apply` | signed in | `{ answers, availability?, consent_email? }`, one answer per question. Answers 201 `{ application }`. Refusals: 409 `not_open` (not open, or past its close), 403 `not_eligible` (the caller's role), 409 `programme_full`, 409 `already_applied`, and 400 for missing answers. `consent_email: true` records the time and `consent_version`. The member is notified (`testing_applied`). |
| `POST /api/testing/applications/:uid/withdraw` | the applicant | An applied or picked application becomes `withdrawn`, which frees a pick's place. Another member's is 404; one already withdrawn or declined is 409 `not_withdrawable`. |
| `GET /api/testing/summary` | signed in | The Studio card (D814): `open_count`, what the caller could apply to now, and `picked`, the programmes they were picked for. |
| `GET /api/partner/testing/programmes` | a partner | `items`: their firm's programmes (`owner_partner_id`), each with `places_left`, `picked_count` and `applicant_count`. |
| `POST /api/partner/testing/programmes` | a partner | A draft for their firm (201): product name, logo key, summary, tester tasks, time needed, the four dates, `places` (1 to 10,000), `eligible_roles` (one or more of founder, partner, investor, exploring, advisor) and up to 5 `questions`. Bad input is 400 `invalid_request`. |
| `PATCH /api/partner/testing/programmes/:uid` | a partner | The same fields, on a draft or on one HQ sent back, which becomes a draft again. Any other status is 409 `not_editable`. |
| `POST /api/partner/testing/programmes/:uid/submit` | a partner | A draft is sent to HQ (`submitted`). Anything else is 409 `not_submittable`. |
| `GET /api/partner/testing/programmes/:uid/applicants` | a partner | `{ programme, items }`: each applicant's name, role, answers, availability, `consented`, status and dates, without the withdrawn ones. Never an email address. |
| `POST /api/partner/testing/applications/:uid/pick`, `…/decline` | a partner | Allowed while the programme is open, testing or closed. Pick takes an applied or declined application; past the places it is 409 `programme_full`. Decline takes an applied or picked one. The member is notified (`testing_picked`, `testing_not_picked`); nobody is emailed. |
| `GET /api/admin/testing/programmes?status=` | Super Admin | Every programme, with `owner_name` (Axal VC, or the partner firm), optionally one status. An unknown status is 400. |
| `POST /api/admin/testing/programmes/:uid/approve`, `…/reject` | Super Admin | `{ note }`, required to reject. A partner's submitted programme becomes `approved` or `rejected`, with `review_note`. Anything else is 409 `not_reviewable`. Whoever created it is notified (`testing_programme_approved`, `testing_programme_sent_back`). |
| `POST /api/admin/testing/programmes/:uid/open` | Super Admin | HQ's from draft, a partner's only once approved; otherwise 409 `not_openable`. |
| `POST /api/admin/testing/programmes/:uid/close` | Super Admin | An open or testing programme becomes `closed`; otherwise 409 `not_closable`. |
| `POST /api/admin/testing/programmes`, `PATCH …/:uid`, `GET …/:uid/applicants`, `POST /api/admin/testing/applications/:uid/pick`, `…/decline` | Super Admin | The partner's routes, on HQ's own programmes (`owner_kind = 'hq'`). A partner's programme answers 404 here. |

Mail's testers list is `testersAudience(env, uid)`: the picked applicants who consented, with active accounts. Each comes with `email`, `name`, `first_name` and `consent_version`. D811's link (#2257) mounts it as `testers:<uid>`. Part 2 of #2258 adds that mount and the two `/api/mail-link/testing` reads.

### Legal templates Worker contract (D717, #1782)

Under `/api/admin/contracts` (`admin_contracts.ts`). Each template's kind and Axal party are stored on its row (migration 479), and NULL means "from the slug" (`services/templateFormat.ts`). `GET /templates/store` and `GET /templates/store/:slug` add `doc_kind`, `axal_party`, the effective `kind` and `party`, `format_source` (`row` or `slug`) and `format_fixed`; the single read also adds `format_options` (the storable kinds, the parties, the partyless kinds, the slug's own pair and, since #1852, `entity`: the entity the slug names when the chosen kind takes a party). `POST /templates/store` and `PUT /templates/store/:slug` take `doc_kind` and `axal_party`: an absent key keeps the value, `null` resets it, and a bad pair is 400 `kind_invalid`, `kind_not_drawn`, `party_invalid` or `kind_party_mismatch`. On a #1781 notice or booklet slug, setting either is 409 `format_fixed`.

Behind `requireHqAuthoring`: `POST /published-templates/:slug/publish` `{ version }` snapshots that one row with both columns and writes `contract_template_published` (409 `published_version_changed` or `template_has_no_body`; 404 `template_not_found`); `POST /published-templates/:slug/withdraw` removes that publication and writes `contract_template_withdrawn` (404 `not_published`); `POST /published-templates/push` sends the recorded publications to every branch and writes none, answering one row per branch with its state. The library-wide `POST /published-templates/publish` keeps working with no screen caller. `GET /templates/outside` (`requireAdmin`) lists the bundled keys a sender still sends as the bundled file (`BUNDLED_SENT_DIRECTLY`), each with the row it counts under; empty since D758 PR 2.

`preview.pdf` and `POST /envelopes/preview` print the effective pair. A send records the pair on `esign_envelopes`, the publication's for the contract send and the active row's otherwise, and signing and the DocuSign source read the envelope's values. PR 2a added the `contracts.js` methods. Since #1854 the phone preview (`TemplatePreviewPhone.jsx`) reads `preview.pdf` once per open, and shows `X-Axal-Renderer`'s fallback sentence. Since #1852 the template editor (`contracts/TemplateEditor.jsx`) calls `POST /templates/render` with the draft's pair, `publishOne` and `withdraw`. #1851 adds `counsel_draft` to the store list, and `pushed_at` and, on a branch that hit the deadline, `timed_out: true` to the push. The Templates tab (`contracts/TemplateCatalog.jsx`, mounted by `AdminTemplates.jsx` on `/admin/contracts?tab=templates`) reads `templates` (the store list), `outside()` and both thumbnails (`thumbnailBlob`, `outsideThumbnailBlob`), and calls `push()`.

PR 1b, behind `requireAdmin`:
- `GET /templates/store/:slug/thumbnail.png` and `GET /templates/outside/:key/thumbnail.png`: page 1 as a PNG drawn once per version by Browser Run's screenshot and kept in `FILES`; `private, max-age=86400`, the key as the ETag, 304 on a match; 503 `thumbnail_unavailable` when it cannot be drawn, writing nothing. Binary, outside the typed drift surface.
- `POST /templates/render` also takes `doc_kind` and `axal_party` (a present key wins, `null` is from the slug; the checks and codes of a save) and answers `fields: [{ key, filled_by }]` (`send` or `sender`). `POST /templates/render.pdf` takes the same body and answers the draft's PDF with `X-Axal-Renderer` and `no-store`; 413 `body_too_large` over 256 KB. Binary, outside the typed drift surface. Since #1853 its caller is the template editor's Pages view (`contracts/DraftPages.jsx`, through `contractsApi.renderPdfBlob`).
- D758 (#1864): `GET /templates/generated` answers `{ items }`, the ten documents the platform generates in code, each with its library rows, its refusal (`notice_body_missing` or `booklet_part_missing`) and `thumbnail_version`; 503 `generated_unreadable`. `GET /templates/generated/:key/thumbnail.png` draws that document's page 1 as a specimen (bracket figures, no record, no account) under `legal/thumbnails/generated/<sha256 of key>/<specimen sha256 first 16>-<paper_sha256 first 16>.png`; 404 `not_found` for an unknown key, 503 `thumbnail_unavailable` when it cannot be built or drawn.
- `ppm`, `subscription_booklet` and `compliance_manual_code_of_ethics` print the Structure of the Fund page wherever they are drawn: bracketed accounts on a preview, masked accounts on an issued copy.

`GET /api/admin/fund-structure` (`admin_fund_structure.ts`, `requireAdmin`, HQ only): the entity and money map (`services/fundStructure.ts`). A Super Admin's carries each receiving account masked or the resolver's sentence; a plain Admin's carries "Only the Super Admin can see or change payment instructions." and reads no account. #1855: the model also carries the eight numbered `rows`, the `map` both drawings print, `signing` and `rails`, and, for the Super Admin only, `accounts` (each entity's active accounts masked to the last four, from the registry's list; null if it cannot be read). `no-store`; 503 `fund_structure_unreadable`. Its screen caller is the "Structure of the Fund" sheet on `/admin/contracts` (`?sheet=structure`, `pages/admin/contracts/FundStructureSheet.jsx`): a door in the desk header on every tab, read only once open, absent on a branch, with a "Bank accounts" link for the Super Admin.

The canvas is `design/canvases/integrated/Legal Documents · Templates.dc.html` (#1850; moved from `design/incoming/` on 2026-10-19 when #1854 closed #1782). Where it differs from #1782, the export wins on layout and copy, and the issue wins on data, routes and who may act (D717 decision 1).

### Usage page and HQ allowances (D642, #1435)

`GET /api/usage/storage` (`usage.ts`, `services/storageUsage.ts`) reads the caller's own stored files across all their companies, whatever company the sidebar has selected. It returns `{ total_bytes, files, unknown_size_files, areas }`, with one area per store: `data_room`, `diligence_files`, `research_library`, `message_attachments` and `interview_recordings`. A recording counts toward the member who owns its project. A NULL size counts as unknown, never as 0 bytes, and a failed read marks only that area `recorded: false`.

Both screens are built to the Usage canvas (`Ops · Boards.dc.html`, set `usage`; `us-*` and `hq-allowances` in `Ops Page.dc.html` and `ops-data.js`, committed by #1456). Their figures come from D640 (`ai_budget.ts`), D641 (`ai.ts`: `by_model`, `days`, task labels, `GET /ai/rates`) and the storage read above, never from the canvas's bracketed placeholders.

### Connectors page and Build's "Connect a tool" (D651, #1463)

Connectors replace Integrations (D649). `/integrations` and `/account/integrations` redirect to `/account/connectors` with their query and hash, so a provider's sign-in, which still returns to `/integrations?…` (`integrations.ts`, `calendar.ts`, `linkedin.ts`), opens that connector's own page on what happened (D753, #1876: `/account/connectors/:key`; a Mercury return goes to the hub, stripped). The page reads `/api/integrations` until #1462 (D650) lands `/api/connectors`; the fields only D650 carries read "Not recorded". `IntegrationsPage.jsx` is retired. Canvases: `Connectors Page.dc.html`, `Cycle Action.dc.html` and `Connectors · Boards.dc.html`, in S1's intake for #1461.

**AI assistants (D665, D666, #1496).** StudioOS answers as a remote MCP server at `/mcp` (D784, #2204; `/api/mcp` and every path under it answer 308 to the same path under `/mcp`), with its OAuth 2.1 endpoints at `/api/oauth/authorize`, `/api/oauth/token` and `/api/oauth/register`, and discovery at `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` and `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp`. These are not Hono routes: the Worker's `fetch` sends them to `cloudflare-worker/src/mcp/oauth.ts` first. The SPA's half is `GET /api/mcp/consent/:id`, `POST …/approve` and `POST …/deny`, `GET /api/connectors/assistants` and `DELETE /api/connectors/assistants/:id` (`routes/mcp_consent.ts`). The consent page is `/connect/assistant?request=:id` (D668, #1498): `pages/connect/ConnectAssistantPage.jsx`, mounted signed-in outside the shell's guard. The grants are Connectors' "AI assistants" group (`pages/account/AssistantsGroup.jsx`, `#ai-assistants`), and Record a session offers "From an AI assistant". All of it answers 404 while `MCP_SERVER_ENABLED` is not "true" or `OAUTH_KV` is not bound; live in production since 2026-10-14 (D681).

### Interviews from an AI assistant (D667, #1497)

The interview tools of the StudioOS MCP connector (D665) are
`submit_interview`, `propose_placements` and `structure_with_studioos`, in
`services/assistantInterviews.ts`. They are registered on #1496's
`/mcp` server, behind `MCP_SERVER_ENABLED`, so no SPA route is new.
What they write is reviewed on the Interview Session page's review step
(#1473), which reads `GET /api/founder/validate/sessions/:id/placements`.
That read now labels each run's `source` (`assistant` or `studioos`) and the
assistant's `submitted_via_name`. See `decisions/D667.md`.

### WhatsApp interview channel Worker contract (D754, #1871)

`routes/channels.ts`, mounted on `/api/channels`. The runbook is `documentation/operations/WHATSAPP.md`. `WHATSAPP_CHANNEL` (`off` / `canary` / `on`, default `canary`, #1950) decides who may connect or link: in canary only Super Admins. Routes marked *venture* answer 404 `unknown_provider` to a member who cannot hold a venture (D757 §2.11); a closed route answers 503 `not_available`.
- `GET /` lists each provider's `available` (secrets set and the rollout open to this member), `missing` secret names for an admin, `rollout` for a Super Admin, and for WhatsApp the Embedded Signup ids; each connection with its status and, for WhatsApp, `calls` (why WhatsApp Calling is not offered, #1586).
- **Way A, the member's business number.** `POST /whatsapp/connect` *venture* `{ code, waba_id, phone_number_id? }` finishes Embedded Signup (400 `invalid_request` on malformed ids). `DELETE /whatsapp` and `POST /whatsapp/test` stay open to an existing connection.
- **Consent (#1954, D754 §4–§5).** `POST /threads/:uid/assign` takes `consent?: { ask: true } | { method }`: a WhatsApp chat links and waits. `POST /threads/:uid/consent-request` sends Axal's notice, only when the member presses it, inside the 24-hour window, at most twice, 24 h apart. `POST /threads/:uid/consent { method: verbal | written | pre_agreed }` takes the member's word once the notice was delivered (400 `consent_method_required`). Both refuse 409 `consent_not_offered`, `attestation_not_offered`, `thread_ignored`, `consent_needs_interview` or `already_given`. `GET /threads/:uid` carries `consent` and each voice note's `voice_fetch_by` (the media id's 7-day deadline).
- **Way B, Send to Axal (#1937–#1939).** Every route answers 503 `not_available` until all four `WHATSAPP_AXAL_*` settings are set; a Super Admin's 503 also carries `missing`, the unset names only (#2056). `POST /whatsapp/link` *venture* returns a one-time code and its `wa_link` (at most 10 codes an hour, 429 `rate_limited`); `GET /whatsapp/link` *venture* the newest claim, with Axal's `axal_number` and a plain `axal_link` (wa.me); `POST /whatsapp/link/:uid/confirm` *venture* is Axal's half; `DELETE /whatsapp/link` unlinks. `PUT /whatsapp/link/venture { project_id | null }` *venture* records where recordings go, in `whatsapp_link_settings` (migration 504, #1939 part 3): 409 `whatsapp_not_linked` without a link, 403 `venture_not_yours` for a venture the member's founder account does not own, whether missing or another's; `GET /whatsapp/link` returns it as `venture`, and every unlink clears it. `GET /whatsapp/captures` lists what the member forwarded, with no media id; `POST /whatsapp/captures/:uid/consent { method: pre_agreed | verbal | written }` answers a capture still waiting (404 `capture_not_waiting`).
- **The webhook.** `GET /whatsapp/webhook` answers Meta's `hub.verify_token` challenge (403 `forbidden`, 503 `not_configured`). `POST /whatsapp/webhook` checks `X-Hub-Signature-256` over the raw body before parsing: 401 `unauthorized` unsigned or wrongly signed, 503 `not_configured` with no secrets, 400 `malformed`, 500 `retry` when an event could not be stored. With `WHATSAPP_CHANNEL` `off` it still verifies, then answers 200 `ignored: rollout_off` and stores nothing. `scripts/check-spa-live.mjs` fails the smoke run if an unsigned POST is ever accepted.

### Validate cycle Worker read (D659, #1472)

`cycle_validate.ts`, mounted at `/api/cycles/validate`, is the Worker half of
the Validate cycle in `Cycle Overview.dc.html` (cycle Validate; O1, O1n, O5)
and `Cycle Modules · Boards.dc.html` (MV1–MV6). `GET /overview?project=`
returns #1469's four figures (interviews this month, pain themes, hypotheses
supported, waitlist sign-ups) and six cards. `GET /modules/:slug?project=`
returns one card and its records table. The slugs are `problem-discovery`,
`customer-research`, `ideal-customer-profile`, `market-intelligence`,
`demand-signals` and `experiments`. The SPA pages that draw them are #1474's
(S07), so these are routes for those screens, not screens of their own.
The Spin-Out Lab market page already reads `GET /market-readiness` and
writes `PUT /market-marks/:item` and `POST|DELETE /market-sources`. The other
writes (`/segments`, `/interviews/:id/segment|stage`, `/demand-signals`,
`/waitlist-target`, `/interviews-target` (D685 §5.12), `/competitor-mentions`, `/hypotheses/:id/plan`,
`/evidence-links/:id/strength`) call the same writers as #1471's interview
accept path. See `decisions/D659.md`.
D690 (#1692, migration 460) adds the form writes for the six records, each a
writer in `services/validateWriters.ts` that logs its record and refuses a
path id of another venture with a 404: `POST /themes`, `PATCH|DELETE
/themes/:id`, `PATCH /segments/:id`, `POST /competitors` (a name, one of the
caller's analysed companies or a directory company; 201 when added),
`PATCH /competitors/:id` (watch and positioning), `PATCH
/demand-signals/:id` (a `signal-` record only), `POST /hypotheses` and
`PATCH /hypotheses/:id`. The row actions' writers (merge, split, attach a
quote, Stop, evidence, experiments, a signal's file) are each record's
actions, below.
`founder_validate_sessions.ts` adds `GET /sessions/:id/audio`, one
short-lived signed download per recorded chunk, and `DELETE
/discovery/interview/:id` now calls `services/interviewWriters.ts`'s
`deleteInterview`.
The `experiments` records carry Eadwyn's mark (D424) on each hypothesis as
`provenance`, read by the board's `claimFills`. A failed read sets
`fills_recorded: false` and marks every row `unreadable` (#1523, `D661.md`).
Each module read also carries its list's title (`module.records_title`), the
canvas's columns, and `records.filters` and `records.sorts`, default sort
first (D690 decision 10). Filters are `filter=key:value` pairs:
problem discovery adds `icp:yes`, `need:yes` and `sort=recent`; customer
research filters `icp`, `consent` and `channel` (and still `stage:pipeline`
with `sort=booked` for the recruiting picker); the profile adds `fit:strong|none`
and sorts by evidence; experiments adds `status:retired` (retired claims, only
then), `sprint`, `moved:recent`, `history:last_week` and `history:this_month`,
each applied before paging. Interview rows carry `role`, `pains`,
`duration_min`, `state` (D690 decision 9) and, for a writer, `session_id`.
Beside `records`, `parts` holds what the bottom sections draw, each count over
the whole venture (decision 16): `can_write` on every module; `stats` on
customer research, problem discovery and experiments; problem discovery's
`themes` (writers only) and `unthemed` with `unthemed_total`; experiments'
`fit_gap` and `stats.history_since`. A part follows its store's gate, and a
phrase said without consent reaches a non-writer as
"Quote withheld (no consent to quote)". See `decisions/D690.md`.

### Verdict (D693)

Verdict is Validate's seventh module (MV7). `GET /api/cycles/validate/overview` adds its card only for a viewer `canReadDecision` admits, and every Validate read sends `can_read_decisions`. `GET /api/cycles/validate/modules/verdict` is the Decisions table, and refuses a partner with 403 "Partners do not see decisions." before any read. `POST /api/cycles/validate/decisions?project=` (`cycle_verdict.ts`) records one decision, scoped to the venture or one hypothesis, withheld below five checked pieces of evidence. Each decision's record, activity, actions (add and close a commitment) and Record a decision's form-options are `services/records/verdict.ts`'s, gated by `canReadDecision` to read and `canWrite` to write. The summary CSV reads through the same `services/verdict.ts`. The old `GET|POST /api/founder/validate/decision/:pid` are removed (D704, #1666).

| Canvas | Live route | Component and Worker | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Cycle Overview.dc.html` CO:542, CO:584, CO:610, CO:335, CO:368 (MV7) | `/validate/verdict` (a static line, its guard kept), `/validate/verdict/:id`, `/validate/verdict/new` · founder, admin | `pages/cycles/CycleRoute.jsx` (`slug="verdict"`), `adapters/validate.js`, `verdictForm.js`, `lib/decisionMemoPdf.js`; `cycle_validate.ts`, `cycle_verdict.ts`, `services/verdict.ts`, `services/records/verdict.ts` | CURRENT |

### Cycle records (D690, #1690 and #1692)

`cycle_records.ts`, mounted at `/api/cycles`, serves one record of any cycle
that registers a service in `services/records/index.ts`; Validate, Build,
Raise and Grow do:

- `GET /:cycle/modules/:slug/records/:id?project=`: the record, its sections,
  actions and `next`, and its first activity page;
- `GET …/records/:id/activity?project=&page=`: 20 rows a page, newest first;
- `POST …/records/:id/actions/:key?project=`: a declared action only, JSON or,
  for a file field, `multipart/form-data`; `PATCH` and `DELETE` on the record
  run its `edit` and `delete`;
- `GET /:cycle/modules/:slug/form-options?project=`: the Add forms' choices,
  every other query parameter passed through.

The refusals run in order:

1. no session: 401;
2. a missing or malformed `project`: 400;
3. an unknown cycle, a cycle with no service, or an unknown module: 404;
4. a malformed id: 404, before any read;
5. a missing venture: 404;
6. the module's read gate, or the write gate for actions and form-options: 403;
7. an id not in the module's own store for this venture: 404, as is any id an
   action's body names.

A partner reads a record without its writer-only sections and gets
`actions: []`. Validate's six (`services/records/validate.ts`, #1692) are a
pain group, an interview, a segment, a competitor candidate, a demand record
(`signal-`, `revenue-` or `waitlist-`) and a hypothesis. See
`decisions/D690.md`.

Build's six (`services/records/build.ts`, D690 S18-B, #1694 and #1695) are
gated `read` and `write`, as their module read is (`canReadBoard`, `canWrite`):

| Module | Id | Actions (`POST …/actions/:key`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `solution-design` | the feature's id | `add-risk`, `edit-description`, `scope-out` or `scope-in`, `add-to-roadmap`, `mark-must-have`, `edit` |
| `prototype-mvp` | the must-have's feature id | `add-session`, `add-finding`, `link`, `add-check`, `check`, `mark-blocked`, `mark-done`, `edit` |
| `product-roadmap` | the item's id | `add-dependency`, `move`, `edit` |
| `founding-team` | `h-<holder id>` | `edit-split` (a link), `log-contribution`, `edit` |
| `company-creation` | `step-<n>`, `obligation-<n>` or `lab-<order>-<n>` | `step-`: `mark-filed`, `step-check`, `edit`; `step-` and `obligation-`: `upload-proof` (multipart), `open-proof`; `lab-`: none |
| `operating-systems` | the kind (`crm`) | `reconnect` (a link), `edit` (Choose connector), `disconnect` |

Each edit-style action runs the same function as its route in `cycle_build.ts`
(`POST /features`, `POST /must-haves`, `PATCH /features/:id`, `POST /risks`,
`POST /roadmap`, `PATCH /roadmap/:id`, `POST /founders`, `PATCH /founders/:id`,
`POST` and `PATCH /formation-steps`, `PUT /systems/:kind`), and every write logs
one `build.<module>` row. Build's form-options serve each module's choices, and
Operating systems' per kind add the catalogue providers and `sync_editable`.

Raise's seven (`services/records/raise.ts`, D691 #1699 and #1700) are gated
`write` throughout, so only the venture's owner, an admitted Lab member on
their own venture or an admin reaches them (403 for anyone else, partners
included). Their ids and actions:

| Module | Id | Actions (`POST …/actions/:key`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `fundraising-readiness` | the item key (`data_room`) | `rescore`, or `score` (a form) for `team` |
| `investor-narrative` | the version (`3`) | `duplicate`, `edit` (`PATCH` the record) |
| `pitch-deck` | the share token's id | `revoke` |
| `data-room` | the file's id (its `uid` rides on the record) | `manage-access` (a link), `replace` (multipart), `remove` |
| `cap-table` | `h-<n>`, `e-<n>` or `pool` | `model-a-round` (a link), `edit` for a founder or a SAFE/note |
| `use-of-funds` | the section key (`go-to-market`) | `add-hire`, `edit-split` |
| `capital-relationships` | the prospect's id | `log`, `add-feedback`, `mark-passed`, `edit` |

Each action calls the writer its `cycle_raise.ts` or `data_room.ts` route
calls, and logs one `raise.<module>` row. Raise's form-options serve each
module's Add form; Capital relationships takes `q` for the fund search, read
only from the funds the directory serves. See `decisions/D691.md`.

Grow's seven (`services/records/grow.ts`, D691 #1698) are a feedback (its
uid), a channel (uid), a KPI (`mrr`, `paying_accounts`, … or `custom-<uid>`),
a role (the posting's id), a partner (uid), a market (uid) and an obligation
(its id). Their read and write gates are `canAccessProject`, as
`cycle_grow.ts`'s are: an advisor member reads, without a third party's name,
and gets `actions: []`; applicant names and Close role are the posting's
host's or an admin's. Each module answers `form-options` with its chips.
`POST …/durable-company-building/records/:id/actions/mark-held` takes
`multipart/form-data` for its minutes. See `decisions/D691.md`.

### Raise cycle Worker read (D663, #1476)

`cycle_raise.ts`, mounted at `/api/cycles/raise`, is the Worker half of the
Raise cycle in `Cycle Overview.dc.html` (cycle Raise, O3) and
`Cycle Modules · Boards.dc.html` (MR1–MR7). `GET /overview?project=` returns
four figures (readiness score out of 100, committed against the target,
investors in conversation, months of runway) and seven cards.
`GET /modules/:slug?project=` returns one card and its records table. The
slugs are `fundraising-readiness`, `investor-narrative`, `pitch-deck`,
`data-room`, `cap-table`, `use-of-funds` and `capital-relationships`. The
writes (D691, #1700's second PR, #1751) are `PUT /readiness/:key`,
`POST /readiness/rescore` (`{ items?, since? }`), `POST /narrative-versions`
and `PATCH /narrative-versions/:version`, `POST /deck-links` and
`PATCH /deck-links/:id` (through `createShareLink()`, plan-gated as the share
route is), `POST /holders` and `PATCH /holders/:id` (through `addHolder()` and
`updateHolder()`), `PUT /use-of-funds`, `POST /prospects` and
`PATCH /prospects/:id` (through `createProspect`),
`PUT /data-room/files/:id/checklist` and `PUT /prospects/:id/next-step`. Each
checks every id it takes against this venture (404 otherwise) and logs one
`raise.<module>` activity row per record it writes. The record actions
(duplicate, revoke, add-hire, log, add-feedback, mark-passed, edit, edit-split,
re-score, score, and the data room's replace and remove) are declared on the
records in "Cycle records" above, each through the same writer. #1700's third
PR wrote the writers for duplicate, revoke, add-hire, log, add-feedback and
mark-passed, and the data room's `replaceDataRoomFile()` and
`removeDataRoomFile()`.
Only the venture and admins reach it. The
pipeline, cap table, data room and pitch tools keep their own pages; the
module pages summarise them and link there. The deck share viewer's heartbeat
now sends seconds per slide, which closes the "no per-slide dwell" gap noted
for Pitch below. With `ROUND_CALLS_ENABLED` on, Capital relationships marks the
prospects that responded to a round call (D653) and counts their soft
commitments; it reads `round_call_recipients` and writes nothing. See
`decisions/D663.md`.
