A guard that pins a refusal has to be re-aimed the day the refusal stops being true; with D149's `territory_licences.test.mjs` and `hq_licences_h2h3.test.mjs` that is **five instances across two PRs**, which makes it a class rather than a coincidence: every false claim this programme has shipped had a test holding it in place. **Deliberately not built, each measured:** H13's scope chip as a CONTROL (the page decides what it fetched before the rail runs, so both options produce the same read — the `still_an_admin` mistake D134 named and D138 refused for H9's Move modal), rule 2's per-scope cost multiplier, rule 4's audit row (the rail reads no branch, so logging it as a privileged branch read would write a FALSE entry), and H13's HQ margin (`est_cost_usd` is a cost with no price; H5 already ships that refusal — third time this canvas figure has been refused). **No migration — 269 still free**, and no new `/api/*` method: both fields were already on the payload. **UPDATE 2026-09-17 (#235, D152):** HQ Security stopped denying a store it had. `admin_security.ts` carried one constant with two consumers — *"No guardrail-hit, flagged-output or token-anomaly counter is stored for the AI rails"* — and **two of its three clauses were false**: `ai_usage_logs.safety_score` (migration 040) is written by `recordUsage` on every router call, and `advisor_turn_audit` (migration 043) carries `refusal_reason = 'safety_block'` and `shadow_flagged` with its own index. Worse, the verdict rollup was **already aggregated and already on screen** — `loadAiUsageReport` served it at `GET /api/monitoring/ai-usage` and `AiUsageTab` drew it as "Guardrail safety (llama-guard)" — so the security desk refused a figure the same admin console rendered one click away. **Sixth refusal that outlived its fact** (D129, D131, D140, D147, D150's three rail rows, D151's three zones) and the first where what was denied was visible elsewhere in the product. The rollup was inline inside `loadAiUsageReport`, so a second consumer would have been the **second copy of the SQL**; it is now `aiRouter.loadGuardrailCounters(env, days)` with `loadAiUsageReport` as its first caller — the **twelfth consolidation** (D127, D128, D130, D131, D138, D140, D142, D144, D149, D151). Two stores because they count different things: `ai_usage_logs` holds what llama-guard **thought**, `advisor_turn_audit` holds what the platform **did**, and each half carries its own `available` flag because `advisor_turn_audit` is lazily bootstrapped — an absent table read as "0 turns blocked" is the #204 defect on the worst possible surface. `safe_rate` is **null, not 0**, when nothing was evaluated. The refusal is **narrowed rather than deleted** (D111's pattern): token anomalies (the one clause that was true), *which* guardrail rule fired (computed on every hit, sent in the 422 body, and **no store has a column for it** — a producer with no store, the eighth instance, and the reason H7's per-category rows stay undrawn), and **by branch** (neither table carries a branch column and Analytics Engine has no branch index). `/governance`'s `guardrails` becomes a **pointer**, not a second copy: both payloads land on one page, so serving the block twice would put two renders of one rollup on one screen — the `audit: { total, feed }` idiom already in this file. **Refused with a measurement:** H12's "Passed on retry" column — `safety` routes to llama-guard and has **no alternates**, so a safety call structurally cannot fall back. **Recorded, not fixed:** D105 justified a shared AE dataset on the grounds it is "indexed by `BRANCH_CODE`"; the repo's sole `writeDataPoint` writes the request path and no branch code anywhere — the write-side half of that justification was never built. **No migration — 269 still free**, and no new `/api/*` method. **UPDATE 2026-09-18 (#235, D153):** #235's remainder — H12 frames 1 and 2, the view-as overlay. HQ could read one branch since D108 and had **no screen that showed one branch at a time**; every HQ figure was a platform total or one card in a fan-out. `/overview` and `/admins` now take `?branch=<code>` and answer a deliberately smaller payload — `{scope, branches: [one], branches_coverage}` — with HQ's own roster, ledger, queue and events NOT read on that path, so the overlay is structurally not a filter over a payload sitting behind it. **No new `/api/*` method:** `hqOverview` and `hqAdmins` gained an argument. **Thirteenth consolidation:** `branchByCode` replaces five hand-rolled `branchBindings(env).find(...)` sites and **each caller keeps its own reason sentence** (D117's rule), with `branchRead` answering one branch in the fan-out's own three states. The chrome is SHELL state — `HqViewingAsBar` above `PortalSwitcher`, persisting nothing — and every action under it is **absent rather than disabled**, asserted as the absence of controls. **Stated, not drawn:** H12's Queues zone (twelve `HqEntrypoint` methods, fifteen `branchOps` exports, none a decision feed) and MTD revenue, which is `null` by construction. **The `/governance` refusal is DELETED** — `tenant_view_available` is true — the ninth instance of a guard pinning a refusal that had to be re-aimed the day it stopped being true. **No migration — 269 still free.** | **UPDATE 2026-09-19 (D166): `admin_news.ts` is retired.** Eleven handlers over the same `articles` table, identical paths to `admin_articles.ts`, and one divergence that was not cosmetic — `if (!['approved','in_review'].includes(row.status))` against the canonical `if (row.status !== 'approved')`, whose own comment reads *"No skipping straight from in_review → published, even by an admin."* Both were `requireAdmin`, so no role boundary was crossed; what the twin removed was the RECORDED APPROVE STEP. Six further handler differences were silent losses (no follower fan-out on publish, no revision snapshot or author notice on unpublish, and a narrower cache bust that left the author's own page stale). The module, its `index.ts` mount, the callerless `adminNews` api.js namespace, `bustEdgeCache` and five now-unreachable `NewsNotifyKind` members all go together. `App.jsx`'s `/admin/news` → `/admin/articles` SPA redirect STAYS. **UPDATE 2026-09-19 (D168): HQ can close a data-subject request, which it could not.** `users.deletion_requested_at` was written only by the subject (`settings.ts` request and cancel), read by `admin_security.ts` `GET /overview` against a GDPR Art. 12(3) 30-day clock and rendered amber with an overdue count — and that file's four handlers could not act on one, so the only way a row left HQ's list was the subject cancelling. Migration **272** adds `dsr_requests` (a side table: `users` is at D1's column cap and a request recurs), `POST /api/admin/security/dsr/:userId/close` closes one as `fulfilled` or `denied` behind `requireSuperAdminWriteBar` with a stored reason, and both the ledger row and the open flag move in one `DB.batch` — if either could move alone they would disagree invisibly. **`fulfilled` records that the MANUAL erasure was carried out; this platform performs none**, and `withdrawn` is the subject's own outcome that HQ may not write. The zone had **zero tests** before this, so the clock arithmetic moved into `services/dsrRequests.ts` where it can fail one. **UPDATE 2026-09-22 (D195): the design of record moved — H1–H13 became H1–H34.** The export was replaced with a fresh decode and is a strict superset. **H14** draws the topology stated once (what HQ can read, and through what); **H15** is the platform read from `studioos_metrics` through the SQL API only, one series per branch, where a suspended branch's series **ends** rather than reading zero. **H16–H19** are the Platform and Content consoles; **H20** the people desk; **H21** billing exceptions; **H22** Support as three queues in a tenant × queue matrix; **H23** a `security_events` ledger with sanctions and DR drill drawn Not recorded; **H24** Funds oversight as a cross-branch registry read rather than the LP workspace; **H25** the impersonation bar as global chrome plus the list of what stays on `/admin` on purpose. **H26–H30** are the white-label tier: Kind becomes **Step 1** of the issue flow (Entity shifts to 2, Territory 3, Seats 4, Terms 5, brand kit 6 for white-label only, Contract + Deploy 7 on the White-Label Operator Agreement), the territory picker gains an informational legend because Axal exclusivity never fires against an operator, the ledger and the health cards gain a Kind pill with an All / Axal / White-label filter, a white-label licence record states *"Platform supervised. Brand unsupervised."*, and Content and Support are kind-gated — the brand-approval lane is empty by construction for a white-label and its escalation kinds reduce to Moderation · Seat increase · Other. **H31–H34** settle the domain question in HQ's favour by taking it away: the licence domain strip is **read-only on every tenant**, subsidiary and white-label alike, with one emergency action — Detach, with a typed reason — and no Approve, no Add domain, and no DNS editor for HQ to complete on a tenant's behalf. **The canvas carries that as a correction against itself** (H26 · H32): an earlier draft made Domain an HQ console and it is not — Super Admin stays on `axal.vc` and `app.axal.vc` and binds nothing else, and the brand-kit step collects no hostname of any kind. **Six of these artboards propose retiring live `/admin` consoles and none of them is acted on.** H16/H17 retire the Integration Keys, GitHub Sync, Payments, Promo Codes, Monitoring and Telegram Channels tabs; H18/H19 retire `/admin/assessment` authoring, the Personas taxonomy, `/admin/publications` and `/admin/network-profiles`; H20 retires the `/admin` Users table; H21 retires `/admin` Billing. **That list is six artboards wide, not the three a first reading of it found** — which is worth stating because the smaller number was carried into a plan before it was counted. The instruction these were commissioned under is explicit that `/admin` routes do not retire, and `frontend/test/admin_route_reachability.test.mjs` requires every `/admin/*` route to stay reachable from the sidebar, so the new HQ screens are **additive** and that guard is not re-aimed. Any retirement is its own decision, later. No route, worker or migration moves in D195. **UPDATE 2026-09-22 (#306, D197):** H31–H34's domain strip is real. `AdminLicences.jsx:1333` rendered `d.custom_domain`, a column that existed **nowhere** in `cloudflare-worker/src` or `sql/` — so the strip read "Not recorded" unconditionally for every licence that has ever existed, the same shape migration 279 fixed for `kind` one block over. Migration **280** creates `licence_domains` (one row per licence, `hostname` UNIQUE across the whole table including detached rows, `state` ∈ `pending|verified|detached` with **no `active`** value); `hydrate` batch-reads it onto the list payload and `GET /:uid` also returns `licence_deployments.hostname` so H31's five columns — Platform host · Custom host · State · Certificate · Primary — need no second SPA fetch. **HQ sets no hostname and approves nothing** (H26·H32's own correction): the only control on this tier is `POST /:uid/domain/detach`, on `requireSuperAdminWriteBar` with a reason floor of 10 characters, audited through `logAdminAction` into `admin_audit_log` rather than `licence_events` — SQLite cannot ALTER a CHECK, and migration 266 had to rebuild that table to admit one value, so detaching is an HQ admin act recorded where no CHECK stands in the way. **`state` stops at `verified` deliberately**: making a host serve needs a Cloudflare for SaaS custom hostname on `os.axal.vc`, for which this repository has zero groundwork, and S17's own sentence is what makes the gap harmless — a licence never waits on DNS, members stay on the fallback host the deploy issued. Three plan corrections are recorded in D197: `make-primary` is not built, the S11 count is 6-of-7 rather than the canvas's 4-of-6, and Domain lands as a Settings row rather than the canvas's sub-nav entry. **UPDATE 2026-09-22 (#307, D198):** H26's step 6 — the brand kit — now has a store, and it is **kind-gated**. Migration 281 adds `licence_brand_kits` (`licence_id` UNIQUE, `mark_r2_key`/`mark_mime`/`mark_bytes`, `primary_hex`/`accent_hex`), and four routes sit on `admin_licences.ts`: `PUT /:uid/brand`, `POST /:uid/brand/mark` (multipart), `DELETE /:uid/brand/mark` — all three on `requireSuperAdminWriteBar` — and `GET /:uid/brand/mark`, a plain gated R2 stream on `requireSuperAdmin`. **All four refuse a subsidiary with 409 `not_white_label`**, because `wlCompare` puts a subsidiary's brand at Axal, fixed, and storing one per licence would let the ledger say otherwise. The kit rides `hydrate` — no `api.js` read method — with `brand_kit_available` / `brand_kit_reason` so a database without 281 renders *unreadable* rather than *no kit*. In the SPA the block leaves Step 1 for a **fourth unnumbered tab**, drawn only when `kind === 'white_label'`: making it `STEPS[6]` would draw it on every licence, which H26 forbids in its own words, and a conditional numbered step would renumber the flow per licence. `activationBlockers` gains one `if` — a white-label with no colours cannot activate (H26's *required to activate*), failing **closed** when the store is unreadable and leaving a subsidiary's activation byte-identical. The upload reuses `brand.ts`'s allowlist, 512 KB cap and `sanitizeSvg` through an exported `readUploadedMark`, but **not** its gate or its key: a licence's mark is keyed `licence-marks/<uid>/…`, never by the uploader. The mark is a **stream, not a signed token** — `mintDownloadToken` is one-time and five-minute-clamped, so an `<img src>` would 404 on its second render; the guard proves it by reading the same URL twice. `show_powered_by` is deliberately not stored (nothing in the shell renders a platform credit), and D198 also corrects a fifth stale sentence the plan had not found, on `AdminStudioOverview.jsx`. **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (#309, D200):** canvas H23 says it "completes Y2/H7", so the privileged-action feed on `/admin/security` became H23's monospace ledger (`ts · actor · branch · event · outcome`) over a fifth store, `security_events` (migration 282), with a sixth filter, "Sign-ins and step-ups", and an `outcome` on every row. The two artboards disagree — H7 still draws `Time · Actor · Tenant · Action · Target and reason` — and H23 wins on this page because it names itself H7's completion; D200 records the disagreement. Sanctions and backup stopped refusing stores the platform has, and the restore drill is the one stated absence left. Detail on the Support Security row. **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (#310, D201 and D202):** `/admin/platform` carries canvas H17's three consoles beside H6's zones, and nothing retired. D201 made the scheduler's record true first — every firing leaves a row, including one that found the lease held, and each of the six declared triggers is read against its own schedule. D202 then added **Monitoring** (branch Workers healthy — answering *and* reporting a working database; cron triggers firing; DLQ depth summed over both dead-letter tables; incidents in seven days, with the sentence that no screen enters one; and traffic by branch as an average rate over the window read, never a live rate), **Broadcast** (Telegram channels by state and what each has sent — the chat id reduced to a boolean in the query, no member count drawn — and X, dashed when not provisioned) and **Feature flags** (still no operator store — D203 builds one — but the eight switches the platform does have, listed read-only through `services/platformSwitches.ts`, each asked of the predicate the code that obeys it calls). All of it rides `GET /api/admin/platform/summary`, still super-admin only: no new route and no new `api.js` method. The flags reason it used to give — that what the codebase calls flags is per-user settings — was half false, and is corrected. **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (#310, D203):** the operator switch store D202 said was missing now exists — `platform_switches` (migration 283), one row per switch, **kill-only**: a row can switch a capability off and release its own kill, never switch one on and never lift a deployment's. Its first and only key is `eadwyn_off`. Every advisor route, `checkKillSwitch` and the Platform registry ask one predicate, `advisorKillState` — the deploy variables first, with no database, then the store through a thirty-second reading per database binding that fails open. A new page, **`/admin/platform/switches`** (`PlatformSwitchesPage.jsx`, `guard(['admin'], hqOnly(…))`), reached from the Feature flags zone by one literal `<Link>` because the Platform page may hold no control, shows both halves and throws or releases with a reason and an acknowledgement; `GET /api/admin/platform/switches` (super admin) reads both halves, and `POST /api/admin/platform/switches/:key` (on `requireSuperAdminWriteBar`) writes one `admin_audit_log` row per change. `Flags` and `Overrides` on P7 became real figures, and the summary's `flags_available` / `flags_reason` pair is gone rather than flipped. It reaches HQ's own deployment only; pushing a switch to the branches is filed. **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (#311, D205): the D112 block above says the escalation loop closes; it closed at the API and on no screen.** `api.escalationAnswer` had no caller until D205 put the control on HQ Support (row 170), and HQ Home's note that answering pushes the decision to the branch linked to Content, which could not answer. **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (#311, D206): HQ refuses a white-label's `content` escalation before recording it.** `recordEscalation` reads the licence kind from HQ's own ledger (deployment, then licence uid, then `territory_licences.kind`) for the one gated kind, `content`, and returns a refusal carrying H30's sentence instead of inserting. It fails closed: an unreadable ledger, or a deployment that names a licence HQ does not hold, throws. It never reads the ledger for an ungated kind, so a suspended branch's `other` appeal cannot fail on it. The rule is one function, `escalationKindsFor`, read by HQ, the branch route and the branch drawer. The pull (`licenceForBranch`) now builds its record with the push's `assembleLicenceRecord`, which fixes three ways it had drifted: it now carries migration 265's five fields, lists territories in order, and sends the contract's template version instead of null. ContentPage's brand-desk note said the lane "does not filter on that yet"; it now says what the gate does. Not built: H30's card 1, Content with a tenant switcher on a white-label (#346). **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (#312, D208): the localisation lane says what each submission concerns — as a label, not a link.** Each row of Content's lane renders `subject_ref` as "About {label}" (`LocalisationRow`), or states that no item was named; a note under the lane says the label is the branch's own name for an item in a database HQ cannot open. `admin_content.ts`'s `localisation_reason` is narrowed again, D111's pattern: brand approval, per-subsidiary attribution and the item a submission concerns now exist, and what is still not recorded is the relation — naming an item does not say the submission localises it rather than changes it — so `Localised` stays `null`. **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (#313, D209): H14 — Platform → Topology, what HQ can read and through what.** `/admin/platform/topology` (`hqOnly`), reached from the Deployments zone by one literal link, so the HQ group stays eleven rows; `GET /api/admin/platform/topology` (super admin) answers from `services/topology.ts`. Four zones: who deploys what — three workflows and the root `npm run deploy`, and nothing deploys a branch a second time; HQ's Worker — its bindings, both RPC sides with the uncalled methods dashed, Cloudflare Access on its two KYC routes, and the three screens that write Worker secrets; one box per branch, from the registry and the live read Deployments already makes; and what every Worker shares — the Analytics Engine dataset and what does not come from it, the gateway with no per-branch metadata, the tail consumer. Every structural fact is held by `topology_d209.test.ts` to the file that decides it. `liveChip` moved to `lib/deployTimeline.js` beside a lifted `residencyLine`. One new `api.js` method, `hqPlatformTopology()`; no migration. **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (#314, D210): H15 — Analytics, one line per branch.** `/admin/analytics` (`hqOnly`), reached from HQ Home's Accounts tile by one literal link and lit under the Home row, so the HQ group stays eleven rows; `GET /api/admin/hq/analytics?range=8w|quarter|year` (super admin — grouped by branch it is every branch side by side). Signed-in accounts per week from Analytics Engine, one line for HQ's own deployment (HQ-held) and one per registered branch, including a branch with no traffic; a code the store recorded that the registry lacks is flagged unregistered only when the registry was read. A missing week is a gap with its reason — the row cap, a week older than the store, no rows, a week before the line began — never a zero, and a suspended branch's line continues, dashed, because suspension freezes writes and not sign-ins. The KPI reads the last complete week and refuses its four-week delta when the two weeks count different lines. Activation, token spend and revenue by stream arrive from the server as Not recorded with their reasons (#354, #358). **D483 (#1025):** median approval age is measured — referrals on HQ's own line and every branch's, over the branch RPC `approvalAges()` that runs the read S15 uses (durations only; one median over every decision across lines), and escalations to HQ on `hq_escalations`; LP and cohort applications stay Not recorded with S15's reasons. **D486 (#1025):** activation is measured. Each line's accounts are grouped by creation week, with these steps: signed in, terms accepted (`tos_v1`), and active in days 8 to 14. Accounts from an accepted invitation are counted beside the funnel. Branches answer over the RPC `activation()` (counts only), and the lines that answered are added together. Not narrowed by the view-as overlay, and says so. The benchmark pipe gains `active_accounts_week`, medianed only across branches reporting the same Monday; `aeSql` now reads a malformed answer as unreadable rather than empty. One new `api.js` method, `hqAnalytics()`; no migration. **UPDATE 2026-09-24 (#368, D211): H15 tells a failed read from an unreachable store.** A metrics read that failed is marked `unreadable` and drawn as Unreadable with a retry; a Worker with no read credential stays "Not recorded" and offers none. A line's gap reason is now its last complete week's, not the first one met walking from the oldest week; the chart's foot names every reason once, with the row cap's size on the cap's own note; and a line's first week is stated only when the read can see where it began. The four-week change is refused across a week holding a line's first request, and for the reason that holds that week — the row cap, the store's floor, or a change in who is counted. A ninth branch sharing the first branch's colour is said on the page, and the rail tells reading, failed and answered-with-nothing apart. The code `hq`, which is HQ's own line in the metrics store, is reserved in every copy of the branch-code rule, the deploy route and both workflows. HQ's branch overlay reads the backlog's `count` (#365). No new route and no new `api.js` method; no migration. **UPDATE 2026-09-24 (#315, D213): H16 — four consoles, summarised where they are read.** Platform gains four read-only panels in a grid before Scheduled jobs, in the artboard's order — Integration keys, GitHub Sync, Payments catalog, Promo codes — each read from the store its console writes, each in its own state, and each ending in one literal link to the console where anything changes; nothing retires. Integration keys: for each of the twelve managed providers, held as a Worker secret, in the database, not at all, or unknown because the key table could not be read (never "unconfigured" for a table nobody read), and when it was last set, from the console's own successful saves, a removal after the last save cancelling the date; no client id reaches the page. GitHub Sync: whether the token is set, the repository only when both halves are set (the mirror's own rule, not the console's display default), Support's 24-hour window, and the latest attempt per ticket with no title. Payments catalog: the publishable key checkout is served, masked by the one mask the Payments console now also calls; products and prices from the mirror, never through `getCatalog`, which calls Stripe when the mirror is empty; a price list that does not parse is counted as unreadable, never as no prices; the webhook's last delivery and its not-2xx count over 24 hours from the request log. Promo codes: each code's terms and one state by one rule (`promoState`), whose expiry test checkout now shares, and Revenue stopped counting expired codes as redeemable. The GET runs no schema bootstrap and makes no network call. Operator consoles keeps Monitoring and Telegram, so each console is linked once. The four claims that a key could be revealed are corrected, and Support's footer links Platform instead of saying this console is not built. No new route and no new `api.js` method; no migration. **UPDATE 2026-09-24 (#316, D214): H18 and H19 — Content draws the pipeline as six lanes and summarises three consoles.** Content's pipeline becomes H19's board — Draft, Review, Localisation, Brand approval, Scheduled, Published — built from the same two by-status reads the page already made, each part naming its store, so a lane cannot disagree with the block beside it and "published" keeps its two meanings. Localisation has no source and says why (#355); Brand approval counts open content escalations through D204's one open-escalation statement, and past its ceiling says it is not fully counted while still drawing the oldest rows it read; Scheduled is `approved` awaiting its publish. **D484 (#1025):** an approved article can carry `publish_at` (migration 346, set by `POST /api/admin/articles/:id/schedule` from the review page); the per-minute cron publishes it when it passes, through the manual publish's own path, and H6 shows how many are timed, how many wait for a manual publish, and the soonest times in UTC. A lane with an unreadable part has no total, never a smaller one. Three read-only panels follow, each read from the store its console writes and each ending in one literal link to it: Assessment Studio (every game with its own chapters, archetypes, active questions and completed runs, the runs in their own read; "live on N branches" was stated, not drawn, until **D487 (#1025)**. Now `POST /api/admin/content/games/publish` pushes every published HQ game to every branch over the RPC `publishGames()`, and the summary reads each branch's holdings and completed runs over `gameHoldings()`), the Personas taxonomy (the twelve personas the code defines, with the primary tags active accounts hold, tags outside the set reported, and the footing beside it), and the Advisors & Partners deck roster (in the deck's own order, each row marked with what the deck does with it — profile, names list, counted or archived — through `services/decks/deckRoster.ts`, the one rule the deck and the summary both call; no bio, photo key or LinkedIn URL reaches the page). The GET runs no schema bootstrap. Nothing retires: `/admin/assessment`, the Personas and Network profiles tabs and `/admin/publications` stay where they are, reached by literal links (`/admin?tab=network-profiles`, never `/admin/network-profiles`). Every Demo Day deck built from live data printed "ADVISORS & ADVISORS"; the worker and the dev mirror now say "ADVISORS & PARTNERS", the canvas's own name for the roster, and a guard holds the three copies equal. No new route and no new `api.js` method; no migration. | **UPDATE 2026-09-24 (#317, D221):** H20 lands on `/admin/accounts`, headed **Team**, in the artboard's order: the holder console, the Team table, H20's two action cards, then the Admin Console's Users panel. Nothing retires: the directory stays, and every `/admin/*` route stays reachable. The cards (`pages/hq/HqTeamActions.jsx`) keep the canvas's names verbatim and replace its notes, each read against the route that performs the act — the canvas said the target sees a banner, that both parties are notified, that Admit to Lab lands on Programs, that grants are checked against seats and that Approvals decides; none of that is true, and the rows now say what gates each act, where it is done and whether Security records it. "View as a role shell" stays in the HQ card, marked as every admin's, because it is. The directory stops drawing View As and Disable where they can only refuse — on an administrator's row unless the viewer holds the elevation, and on the viewer's own row — through one rule, `lib/accountControls.js`, used by the row and the drawer. The Role badge stops saying the admin role changes only through SQL. Two worker defects close: Extend re-runs D133's admin-target guard (it re-ran only the holder guard), and the elevation transfer takes a typed reason of ten characters, refuses a deactivated successor, and records both halves through `logAdminAction` so Security's feed names who received it and why. The plain-grant branch D133 left unreachable is gone; an active set that reads empty mid-request refuses (`no_active_holder`) instead of granting into the gap. No migration; no new route or `api.js` method. **UPDATE 2026-09-25 (task 354, D266): the statement ledger has its input, and what it says is "unmeasured".** `reportUsage` was the ledger's only designed input and had no caller, so `subsidiary_usage_reports` was never written and every statement HQ drew said the branch "has not reported". A branch now reports its previous and its current quarter to HQ at 05:10 UTC every morning (`services/usageReport.ts`, gated `!hqCadences`, one quarter per call under a deadline), and every stream in that report arrives NULL — a branch database records no revenue amounts — with the token stream's inference cost carried as its basis. So H5's statements read "reported, unmeasurable" rather than "not reported": a truer state, not a fuller one. `revenueSummary` and `promoCeiling` are retired from the RPC surface: nothing called either, `branchRevenueSummary` stays as the report's own source, and the ceiling is pushed after every save. Corrected on the way, because each had outlived its fact: HQ Home's revenue rail row and footnote ("no branch has sent one yet"), the H12 MTD tile's server reason, `admin_revenue`'s "no subsidiary-statement store exists" (two do), Revenue's "draw one" (no control draws a statement — filed, not built) and "an issued figure is reported by the branch" (nothing writes one on either tier), and the promo push reason that promised a later deployment would receive the ceiling. No migration; no new route or `api.js` method. **UPDATE 2026-09-26 (task 414, D282): the canvas gains H35–H38, the navigation layer that reaches the older consoles.** H35 the placement map — 37 entries, every old sidebar row and all 14 Admin Console tabs mapped to a tier, a row and a form; H36 sub-navigation strips on Platform (nine items) and Content (seven) that keep the row lit after landing in the old Admin Console; H37 the Workspaces launcher and a ⌘K that indexes only what H35 places; H38 "View as" redrawn as Preview shell. A strict append of four hunks. Its H16–H21 changelog rows still say "Retires /admin …"; nothing retires (D195 §2, D282). No route, no migration, no `frontend/src` change. **UPDATE (D634, #1392, the Ops canvas's Q4–Q6):** a new address, `/admin/revenue/disputes/:disputeId` (`pages/hq/DisputePage.jsx`, HQ only), plus the review comments of `/admin/articles` and the KYC and partner-profile review windows of `/admin`. Q4: Delete beside Resolve, sent only from a confirmation that says the author loses the comment (D617's rule; refusals now carry sentences). Q5: one dispute read live from Stripe (`GET /admin/billing/disputes/:id`), opened from the Disputes rows of Billing exceptions; "Stripe is not configured" and "Stripe could not be read" are separate states; nothing is stored, so no stale copy. The 5-business-day reply is a commitment, not a field: `contact.ts` stores submissions and nothing measures or promises a response time.  **UPDATE 2026-09-17 (#239/#240, D141): `/spinout-lab/brief` is rebuilt as the four-page Programme Brief, and the ask's own notation is what shaped it.** The design artifact carries **76** `{{ }}` bindings (the canvas rendering its own data) and exactly **six** single-brace dotted tokens (`{brief.generated_at}`, `{brief.year}`, `{cohort.name}`, `{cohort.start_date}`, `{cohort.close_at}`, `{cohort.places}`) — which is what "a backend that auto-updates every `{}` bracket element" names, and task #239's title was exact. Those six, and only those six, come from a new public `GET /api/spinout-lab/brief`; five of them read **no table at all** (wall-clock arithmetic in `COHORT_TZ`, which is why the fixture deliberately creates no cohort tables) and the sixth falls back to the product default, which IS the operative number until an operator overrides it. About twenty more bindings already had one shared source the Lab pages read, so the brief reads `lib/spinoutLabArsenal.js` rather than copying it; the remaining ~50 are the programme's own prose and live in the new `lib/spinoutBrief.js` as content in git — **a D1 table for those would be D129's seat store and D140's adjustable dates a third time.** The page that was here was NOT static, a claim corrected on the record: it read live stats through the `useSpinoutStats` hook, which a grep for `api.` does not see — what it lacked was the artifact's whole structure and any route of its own. Nothing derivable is typed: the heading *"Nineteen working tools. Count them."* is `numberWordCap(TOOL_COUNT)` and the test refuses the literal in source as well as asserting the rendered count; the twenty-eight days are `COHORT_WEEKS × 7`. Every instant is formatted in the zone the **route** names rather than one the page assumes (`lib/zoneTime.js`'s third caller). The canvas's nine mini-charts are invented figures — the canvas says so — and the brief is read by exactly the person who has no account, so each ships under `EXAMPLE_LABEL` or not at all. A failed read and a month with no open window are two different sentences. **The latent finding this earned an assertion for:** the SPA computes the cohort window itself (`resolveOpenCohort`, which the marketing hero quotes) in parallel with the worker's `resolveApplicationTarget`, which this route answers from — two implementations of one rule in two languages. Measured across 48 probes they agree on every one, so it is drift risk and not a live defect; the test now pins them equal by importing both real implementations. PDF export is print CSS (`@page { size: letter portrait }`, one break per page), by the owner's call; the document stays white in both themes because a dark page that prints white is two documents under one URL. `BriefDocument` is exported pure and prop-driven so both states can be rendered and read — `renderToStaticMarkup` runs no effect, and the loading state is not the one that has to be right. **One new `/api/*` method with its route in the same commit; no migration — 267 stays free.** |

Migration 235 adds the four columns. **Three backend additions:** `/events` now reports `sources[]` so one unreadable source degrades the agenda instead of 500ing it (it used to blank the page with no way to say which source failed); events carry `pushed_to` read back from `calendar_sync_records`, which had recorded every push since Task #52 and was never read; and `expert_booking` rows are readable at last. **Canvas vs code, recorded rather than silently resolved:** it says `expert_booking` is "produced by nothing" (it is produced — the write was broken — so the page draws six kinds, not five), and it draws a push control on a Calendly event, which `PUSHABLE_KINDS` refuses. **Not drawn, for want of a store:** a provider-level sync failure is not persisted anywhere, so the rail's "Sync failed" state is only reachable from a sync the reader just ran. |

Hashed 14-day token (only the SHA-256 hex is stored, the raw token is returned once), one pending invitation per address enforced by a partial unique index, accept bound to the invited address, resend issues a NEW token and restarts the clock, revoke keeps the row so the address can be re-invited. The invitee's page is `/company/invitations/accept` (`AcceptCompanyInvitePage.jsx`, public, bounces to sign-in with a `?next=` carrying the token). `email_sent: false` is a normal 200 — with no Gmail credentials the page hands the link over once instead of flashing success. `api.addCompanyMember` stays for the surface that means it (`CompanyProfilePanel`'s "Add team member", which says the account must already exist). Parent spec for the three Rails and for Detail Layer Canvas. **UPDATE 2026-09-02: the six overviews are reachable, and the rail is one component.** The overviews had all been built and contract-tested, then displaced — a rebuild took a desk's route slot, the desk was re-mounted on whatever path was free (`/build/discovery`, `/execution`, `/build/team`, `/signals`) and the sidebar row was never pointed back, so five of six workspaces landed on a section page. Each workspace now has a ROOT that renders its overview for a founder and keeps today's behaviour for everyone else; `?mode=workspace` still opens the detailed editor. The legacy mounts are unchanged and still live. Section pills are `<Link>`s to real routes — three desks had shipped them as in-page anchors, two of which named sections that did not exist. Layout: `fullWidthSurface` and `flushSurface` were two hand-typed path arrays matched exactly, and `/grow/focus` was missing from both (the whole of "Grow doesn't fit"); they are one `FOUNDER_FULL_BLEED` list now, and the Build section pages no longer sit inside `FounderWorkspacePage`'s 1440px frame. **The rail:** twenty-seven hand-built copies under seventeen names collapse into `ui/WorkerRail` — mode, coverage, usage, safety. Usage is real (`GET /api/ai/me/spend`); there is deliberately no model or per-run cost, because `ASSIST_SURFACES` keys a surface to an aiRouter task class and no founder workspace runs one, so a model named here would be a model on a page that never calls one. Still open from A1: the mode switch, the model card, the 44px collapsed spine (every host is a grid item in a fixed 270/286px track), and the proposal anatomy — cost before the run and a receipt after, accept/edit/discard, amber strike-through with a second confirm, provenance mark on accepted output. **UPDATE 2026-09-27 (D420–D422):** the desks read the stores their zones already had (D420, D421), and three of the legacy mounts that were a second address for a desk now redirect with the query kept — bare `/execution` → `/build`, `/build/discovery` → `/validate`, `/signals` → `/research`. The editors behind them (ExecutionPage, DiscoveryPage, SignalsPage, NetworkPage, the Raise `?mode=workspace` editors) stay mounted: no canvas zone yet carries their writes (D422 lists them). **UPDATE 2026-09-27 (D424):** the A1 anatomy the four desks with proposal bands were missing. Validate, Build, Raise and Grow pass `fills`, so their rails draw the mode switch their bands were gated on (Build, Raise and Grow had no switch anywhere, so no band on them could run); each prints its own sentence from `ASSIST_SURFACES.workspace.desks`, and Network and Research draw no switch and say why. Every desk band quotes the founder's own average for its task before a run (`workspaces/RunEstimate.jsx`), and the Validate board marks the claims Eadwyn supplied from `fill_provenance`. Still open: the spine's dot and vertical spend (the rail's own files), and the amber overwrite confirm, which no fill needs yet. **UPDATE (D510):** the Friday retro draft is the `build/retro` surface on the Build cadence card, and every zone-draft run records the page it was asked from, so a band's runs count on that page. The pre-run estimate stays the caller's average for the task (`RunEstimate`); it is not a per-page figure. **UPDATE 2026-10-05 (D551, #1144):** the Network desk (A6) reads a contact's `strength` (strong, warm or cool) and `organization` (migration 386, set by `PUT /api/contacts/:uid` for the owner only) and the contact list's follow-up rollup (`next_task_due`, `open_tasks`, `last_reply_at`, one statement). `/network/relationships` edits both per row and shows the follow-up and last note; `/network/organizations` groups the set field and names each group's strongest tie. Network now passes `fills`: its two bands draft over `network/re-engagement` (the founder's cold contacts, coldest first, on that contact's project) and `network/intro-email` (the founder's accepted introductions). "Who should meet whom" stays Not recorded: no store pairs two of a founder's contacts. |

Canvas designs a Get-Paid setup screen with three states, an **in-platform invoice document** (masthead, bill-to, engagement reference, line items carried from the accepted quote, VAT, totals), and a 4-state action rail. **CORRECTION + FIX 2026-08-29.** This row described the FastAPI in `backend/`, which is Replit-dev-only and never deployed. On the production worker `POST /engagements/:id/invoice` set `status = 'invoiced'` and `invoice_id = 'stub-<uid>'` and nothing else; D1's `engagements` has no `stripe_invoice_url` and no `stripe_invoice_id` column, and `engagementDto` is a bare row passthrough — so all three UI references were dead: two invoice links that could never render and a guard that never hid the button (a second click 409'd). The engagement was left permanently marked **invoiced with no invoice in existence**, which is worse than a missing feature: the record asserted something untrue about money. **Fixed** by migration 188 (`engagement_invoices`, `invoice_number_seq`), `services/engagementInvoices.ts` and an in-platform invoice document in `NeedsBoardPage.jsx` — which is what the canvas asked for and needs no Stripe, so it does not reverse task #138's removal of Stripe Connect. Numbers come from a dedicated counter, not from parsing the last number back, and a void invoice keeps its number. **Not built:** payment collection. There is no payment rail; `paid` records that the partner says they were paid out of band, and the document says so to both sides. Still to do from the canvas: the Get-Paid setup screen with its three states. **The row conflated two different Stripe surfaces; they are not the same and only one exists.** (a) WELLBEING EXPERTS have REAL Stripe Connect: `wellbeing.ts` `POST /experts/me/stripe/connect` creates an Express account, requests transfers + card_payments, returns an account-onboarding link and stores `stripe_account_id`, and the `experts` table carries real `stripe_charges_enabled` / `stripe_payouts_enabled` columns — so the Charges/Payouts `<dl>` on `/wellbeing/expert-dashboard` is backed by live data, not a placeholder. The canvas's three-state Get-Paid setup screen IS buildable there, and the three states map onto the real ones (no account / onboarding incomplete / enabled). (b) MARKETPLACE PROVIDERS have none: `needs.ts` `GET /providers/me/stripe` returns `connected: false, detail: 'stripe_connect_not_configured'` and the onboard route 503s with the same reason. Those are HONEST stubs — they name their own absence — which is why they are not on the defect list above; the invoicing path was the one that claimed something untrue. A Get-Paid screen for marketplace providers cannot be built until someone decides whether Connect returns for them at all (task #138 removed the payouts backend deliberately). |

The 2026-09-07 artifact re-export decodes byte-identical to `canvases/integrated/Help Center.dc.html`, so nothing about the design changed either. Decision taken on it: **`/help` becomes the Help Center composed over this corpus, and `/docs` redirects into it** the way D39 handled `/tickets` — no article store is being added, because the content is already structured and role-filtered and markdown would lose that. Task #103. **Task #103 shipped 2026-09-07 — composition and routing, no article store.** `/help` mounts this corpus (`HelpCenterPage`, formerly `DocsPage`); the ticket tracker that used to hold `/help` moved to `/help/tickets` and its `<h1>` changed from "Help Center" to "Support tickets", because two pages claiming the same name is what made this canvas look unbuilt. `/docs` redirects with **hash and query preserved** — the hash IS the article, and eight deep links across six components address one that way. **Built:** the hero ("How can we help?"), the search moved into it with five suggested queries, a "Browse by what you are doing" grid built from `visibleSections` so a new section file appears without a second list to keep, an in-column result list, and a "Still stuck?" contact block that now offers three channels. **Still refused, same reasons:** "Popular this week" (no view counts), "Did this answer it?" (no feedback store), "Where this lives" / "Open the surface" (no per-article `surface` route on ~98 subsections), "Applies to" (**zero** sections carry a `roles` array, not 1 of 98 — `sections/admin.js` is the only file that has one and it was deliberately dropped from the manifest by `2c38e60b3`, 2026-05-22, so `adminOnlyAnchors()` returns an empty set and the `/…/admin/*` guard is inert; left as found, since re-importing it reverses someone's decision). `frontend/test/help_center_contract.test.mjs` pins all four absences. **Five live defects closed in the same pass, every one an address nothing declared or a route nothing read:** `/support?topic=` was a 404 from four pages' error states (now `/help?q=<topic>`); the advisor's `exploreDocs` built `/docs/<section>/<sub>`, a path segment where the corpus wants a hash, so every "Read <topic>" CTA 404'd (now `/help#<anchor>`); `TicketsPage` never read `useParams`, so `/help/<id>` — emitted into Slack by `tickets.ts:147` — silently showed the list; three `ticket_update` notifications linked `/help` for a notification naming one ticket (now `/help/tickets/<id>`); and the pre-KYC allow list was an exact match on `/help`, so an un-KYC'd investor clicking "Open ticket" was bounced to `/kyc` — cut off from the one channel that could unblock them (now the whole `/help` subtree). **`CustomerChatWidget` is mounted at last**: built under Task #7 (IG) with a complete Slack round-trip and referenced nowhere, because its docblock delegated the tier check to "the Help Center help panel" that was never written. `lib/customerChat.js` is that gate, mirroring the worker's `isEligible` with both copies pinned by the contract test; both files are in `canvases/integrated/`. `/help` is now H1: a lavender search band, the areas as two-column cards with tinted icons and four guides each, the reader's open tickets (`GET /api/tickets`; an admin sees every member's), and "Still stuck?" with Open a ticket (`/help/tickets?new=1`, which opens the form), Message the team (chat plans only), Email support and What we can see (`/trust`). Every guide has its own page at `/help/<area>/<guide>` (H2): breadcrumb, reading time from the guide's own words, how-to, the Tips and Common pitfalls callouts, Related, and an "On this page" rail. Still out, each for want of a store: the view-ranked list and the yes/no answer (#1286, S10, D603), and the per-guide surface and licence lines (#1280 step 3). The ticket screens H3 to H7 are #1280 step 4. **UPDATE 2026-10-06 (D603, #1286): the two stores exist, Worker first.** Migration 408 adds `help_guide_views` (anchor, UTC day, count; no reader identity), `help_guide_view_marks` (one counted view per member per guide per day, purged after two days) and `help_guide_feedback` (one yes/no per member per guide, the last wins, an optional "what was missing" with a no). `routes/help_center.ts` serves `POST /api/help/guides/:area/:guide/view`, `GET /api/help/popular`, and `GET`/`PUT /api/help/guides/:area/:guide/feedback` (signed in), plus `GET /api/admin/help/feedback` (admin; counts and texts, no member). The client is `lib/api/helpCenter.js`. One placement table supplies the breadcrumb, tag, area rail, search, popular and admin-answer area names. Stable guide addresses remain unchanged. ## Graduated from `design/incoming/` — part 5 (27 canvases)

The intake queue's pipeline (step 2 of `design/incoming/README.md`) requires a
ROUTE_MAP row per canvas. These two never got one: they arrived in the
2026-09-03/04 batches, after the P−1 audit fixed the 107-canvas corpus above,
and moved into `canvases/integrated/` on 2026-09-07 when the one thing each was
waiting for shipped. Rows written on the move rather than on arrival, which is
the gap, not the intent.

Nothing in the SPA is a general user-to-user inbox; D1 has no `messages`/`threads` table (only `advisor_messages`, `customer_chat_messages`, `assistant_messages`). Needs a store + `messages.ts` worker route. **Wave 4 shipped** the store and the inbox: migration 185 (`message_threads`, `message_thread_participants`, `messages`), `routes/messages.ts`, and `/messages` role-branched inside one route. Membership is the ONLY key — `memberThread()` resolves a thread by uid AND a participant row for the caller, there is no admin override, and every `:uid` handler 404s rather than 403s so a non-participant cannot confirm a thread exists. Unread is derived from `last_read_at` per participant rather than a counter column, so it cannot drift. A thread may pin to a linked object (`subject_type` ∈ introduction, match, engagement, service, session, job) but both subject columns are nullable — a plain conversation is a first-class thread, not a special case. **Not built, and why:** the canvas calls this a *unified* inbox, and there is nothing to unify. D1's only two message-shaped stores are `advisor_messages` (an AI transcript, rows keyed user/assistant/tool/system) and `customer_chat_threads` (the Slack-bridged support desk) — neither is a peer conversation, so folding either in would misrepresent what it is. The inbox therefore starts empty and its empty state names where those two actually live instead of implying they were merged. No per-type filter rail ships until there are threads of more than one type to filter. |

| Pages · Investor Deals | investor-LP | `/deals`, `/pipeline/screening`, `/pipeline/commit`, `/pipeline/transactions` (existing) | `/deals` → `InvestorDealsRoutes` for investors (the ID1–ID4 zone pages plus the bucket board), `DealsPage` for partner/admin; `/pipeline` keeps `PipelineWorkspace` for founders; `/pipeline/screening\|commit\|transactions` redirect to `/deals/screening\|commit\|closing` (D119) | `deals.ts`, `pipeline.ts`, `scoring.ts`, `ic.ts`, `votes.ts`, `dd.ts`, `esign.ts`, `legalcap.ts` | UPGRADE | **Pipeline** adds configurable stages, saved views, velocity, an SLA stale/overdue flag, unassigned-owner surfacing, deal source (incl. Spin-Out Lab) and a passed-deal ledger with a reason taxonomy. **Screening** adds batch runs, rubric configuration and red-flag rules (today `scoring.ts` scores one project at a time with no editable rubric). **Commit** adds quorum arithmetic, conflict/recusal declarations, allocation math and minutes — `ic.ts` records only vote + rationale. **Closing** adds checklist templates, a wire trail and a signature archive. Note the canvas's `/deals/pipeline` etc. would collide with the live `/deals/:dealId` param route. **UPDATE 2026-09-10 (Task #149, canvas ID1):** `/deals/pipeline` is the artboard now, in its own file (`pages/investor/deals/PipelineZone.jsx`) registered in `ZONES` in `InvestorDealsRoutes.jsx`; ID2–ID4 join it there as they land, and a slug with no file still falls through to `InvestorDealsWorkspace`. The route collision the row above warned about did not happen — `/deals/pipeline` and `/deals/:dealId` are both registered and React Router prefers the literal. **What replaced what:** five columns of two-fact cards became the artboard's six-column instrument — Company · Sector · Stage · Ask · In stage · Owner — because the two facts the cards could not carry, how long a deal has sat and who owns it, are the two the artboard's note is entirely about. The strip is four-up over the record: live deals with the total ask, deals past the SLA, unassigned, and on-platform. **The stage vocabulary moved to `lib/dealFlow.js`** (`DEAL_STAGES`, `dealStage`, `dealMoneyExact`) so ID2–ID4 cannot disagree with ID1 about where a deal is; `deals.status` is a CHECKed set of five and the bucket's five stages are not the same five, and `rejected` maps to NO stage at all — a passed deal used to fall through to Commit and sit in the funnel as live. The SLA bands are `slaPreset('standard')`, which is the artboard's own 14/30, read rather than retyped so the strip, the chip row and the AI band cannot drift apart. **Two figures are deliberately not the artboard's.** Its fourth tile counts deals `From the Lab`, and no sourcing channel is stored on a deal — `deals` has no `source` column and the Lab's tables attach to people and applications, so the tile counts on-platform deals instead and the gap is stated in the zone's limits (D56: a tile reading Not recorded because the PRODUCT has no store is design commentary on a customer's screen). And the total ask SKIPS a deal with no recorded ask rather than summing it as zero, and says how many it excluded — arithmetically identical, and the difference between a board-wide figure and one that quietly covers four deals of six. **The AI band is real**: `deals/pipeline` is allow-listed in `routes/research.ts` and gathers deals past the same 14 days, instructed to give two options and no third — assign an owner, or pass and record the reason — and to say first when a deal has no owner at all. Guard: `frontend/test/investor_deals_id1.test.mjs`, which drives `dealStage`/`slaBand` as behaviour and reads the artboard's columns, grid and thresholds out of the canvas. **UPDATE 2026-09-10 (Task #149, canvas ID2):** `/deals/screening` is the artboard now (`pages/investor/deals/ScreeningZone.jsx`), over a new `GET /api/deals/screening` registered before `/:id` and gated on a privileged role. **The finding that shaped it: two of this zone's `unbuilt` reasons were false.** `investorZoneActions` said "no scoring run is stored" and "no rubric is stored"; `score_snapshots` carries SIX dimensions per snapshot — market, team, product, capital, fit, distribution — each with sub-scores and a total beside `total_score` and `tier`, which is exactly the artboard's "Rubric · 6 dims", plus `admin_review_status` and `anomaly_flags`, which is a red-flag store under another name. `POST /api/scoring/score` writes one and locks it for the week. Both reasons are corrected to the narrower true ones: a run is started against ONE project and nothing batches it, and the WEIGHTS are fixed in `services/scoring.ts` with no per-firm store to edit. An unbuilt reason that overstates the gap is as misleading as a control that does nothing — it tells the next reader not to look, which is why the zone had rendered "No deals are currently in screening or diligence" over a live store. **`deals/screening` also left the `excluded` list in `profile_zone_filters.test.mjs`**, which had deferred it for exactly this body work; all four of its chips are live keys (Scored, Rubric, Red flags, Pass reasons) and the investor profile is 17 zones. **A SANDBOX SNAPSHOT IS NOT A SCREENING RESULT** — `is_sandbox` marks a practice run and `official_week` is NULL for those, so they are excluded from every count and the exclusion is printed rather than silent; a rehearsal that moved the desk's numbers would make the numbers untrustworthy. A deal with no snapshot renders unrecorded, never zero. The instrument is the artboard's own — "Pass reasons — the fund's memory" over `GET /api/deals/pass-analytics`, read as a SEPARATE call so one store failing costs its own zone; exactly two of the five taxonomy reasons carry a re-entry condition and the note counts them rather than asserting a number. **The AI band** (`deals/screening`, allow-listed in `research.ts`) drafts a memo against the six dimensions and is instructed never to score a silent dimension zero — a zero is a verdict nobody gave — and never to cite a page or slide, since nothing records which part of a deck a sub-score came from. **Still not built, and stated:** batching, editable weights, and red-flag RULES a person can write (`detectAnomalies` produces flags from fixed heuristics). Guard: `frontend/test/investor_deals_id2.test.mjs`, which verifies the six-dimension claim against the baseline DDL rather than repeating it. **UPDATE 2026-09-10 (Task #149, canvas ID3):** `/deals/commit` is the artboard now (`pages/investor/deals/CommitZone.jsx`), over a new `GET /api/ic/commit-room` registered ahead of `/:uid` so the literal is not read as a uid. **The finding, and it is the same defect ID2 found one zone earlier:** `investorZoneActions` marked `Close vote` unbuilt because "no vote is opened here, so none can be closed", and the file it was written about disproves it in two lines — `POST /api/ic/:uid/vote` moves a decision `draft` → `voting` on the first vote cast, and `PUT /api/ic/:uid` with a `decision` forces `decided` and stamps `decided_at`. Both transitions are stored and served; what is missing is a SCREEN, which is the narrower true claim and the idiom this table already used four rows down ("served by the API; no screen offers the form yet"). The panel it replaced was three DEAL columns — status, total committed, target — under a heading reading "Commit room", and never read `ic_decisions` at all. **RECUSAL IS THE LINE THE PAGE WILL NOT CROSS, and this row's own summary above is what made it tempting:** it says `ic.ts` records only vote + rationale, and that is exactly right — `ic_votes.vote` is `yes | no | abstain`. The ID3 artboard's instrument note is entirely about a partner who RECUSED and was therefore excluded from the denominator, and an abstention is not that: an abstention is a vote cast, the voter counted and declined; a recusal is a declared conflict. Mapping one onto the other would put a false statement about a conflict of interest on a fund's screen, so no vote renders as recused, no denominator is reduced, and the AI instruction forbids the word outright — that surface is the one most likely to reach for it, because every IC memo ever written says it. Conditions, quorum and minutes are absent for the same kind of reason and are returned as explicit unavailable-with-reason rather than omitted. `ic_meetings`/`ic_meeting_attendees` ARE live and give an invited roster with an RSVP joined to a deal, so the artboard's `Eligible voters` tile becomes `In the room` — an invitation is not a voting entitlement, and labelling it as one would invent a governance rule the product does not enforce. **The rationale tile checks the artboard rather than repeating it:** the artboard says `Rationale required`; the column is nullable and the vote endpoint accepts a vote without one, so the tile counts how many votes carry a reason and says the requirement is not enforced. **One scoped read, and only one.** `routes/ic.ts` exports `scopedDecisions`, and both readers — the commit-room route and the `deals/commit` AI gather — go through it, so the predicate migration 219 added exists in one place. That is also why the route lives in `ic.ts` rather than `deals.ts`: a commit-room read in a file that does not import `icDecisionScope` is how that hole gets reopened. `UNSCOPED_ROLES` is `{'admin'}`, so a hard-coded `role: 'admin'` in a scope call turns the predicate into `1=1` and puts every firm's deliberations into one caller's prompt — pinned by the guard. **`deals/commit` also left the `excluded` list in `profile_zone_filters.test.mjs`** (two live keys, two honest `unbuilt` reasons; the investor profile is 18 zones), and the stale line in that deferral saying `api.icList` runs unscoped is corrected in the same pass. Guard: `frontend/test/investor_deals_id3.test.mjs`, 41 mutants. **UPDATE 2026-09-10 (Task #149, canvas ID4 — the last of the four):** `/deals/closing` is the artboard now (`pages/investor/deals/ClosingZone.jsx`), and it added NO endpoint. That is the deliberate part: ID3 ended on the rule that a second copy of a scoped query is how a tenancy hole reopens, and everything this zone needs was already served and already scoped — `api.listDeals` plus `api.esignList`, whose route (`esign.get('/')`) opens its WHERE clause with `esignEnvelopeScope` unconditionally. The zone composes the two and joins on `deal_id` in the browser; the guard pins its API surface at exactly those two calls so a third fails the build. **The finding, which makes three of the four zones:** `Apply template` was marked unbuilt because "no closing templates are stored". `legal_templates` seeds 21 rows with `merge_fields`, `version` and `is_active`, three of them the closing paper this artboard names by name — `safe` (SAFE Agreement), `spa` (Stock Purchase Agreement), `subscription` (Subscription Agreement). The gap is one layer ABOVE the templates: nothing stores a closing CHECKLIST for one to be applied to. `Record wire` was true but imprecise — `capital_calls` carries an amount and a `paid_date`, but that is an LP paying INTO the fund, not the fund wiring OUT to a company, and the reason now says which direction is missing. **THE INSTRUMENT IS DELIBERATELY NOT THE ARTBOARD'S.** ID4 draws a Closing checklist (Item / State / Owner / Note) and no store holds one. `dd_checklist_items` and `diligence_checklists` both belong to DILIGENCE; drawing them here would relabel diligence work as closing work on a document a fund hands to counsel, and D56 forbids drawing the table as rows of "Not recorded". So the instrument is the collection the stage really has — every envelope raised against a deal at closing, with its signature state — and the checklist's absence is stated. **Executed means `status = 'completed'` and nothing else:** a signature count reaching its recipient count is a different fact, and an envelope with NO recipients satisfies that ratio trivially, so it renders unrecorded rather than complete. **Blocking is unbuilt on both sides:** the artboard's note says its blocking item "arrived from the Commit vote", and ID3 established no condition is stored, so no row is drawn as gating a transfer. **The AI band** is instructed never to name a condition (an invented legal impediment in a note to counsel is worse than an omission) and to say plainly that the platform records the movement of money rather than moving it. **THE BUCKET IS COMPLETE, and the workspace is nearly empty.** All four decision panels are gone from `InvestorDealsWorkspace.jsx`, which now draws only the deal-invitation queue; the shaping it did for them — a stage ladder, an SLA band, a money format — went with the last consumer, since the deals array is now read for its LENGTH alone. What it knew lives in `lib/dealFlow.js`, in the version that fixed the defect the old ladder had. `deals/closing` also left the `excluded` list in `profile_zone_filters.test.mjs`, **which is now EMPTY** — every route on all five investor canvases has a filter row, 19 zones. Guard: `frontend/test/investor_deals_id4.test.mjs`, 35 mutants. |

| Pages · Partner Pipeline | partner | `/needs` (existing; `/partner/operations/engagements` for the retainer half) | `/needs` → `NeedsBoardPage` (Browse / My needs / My quotes / Engagements — live-wired); `/partner/operations/*` → mock | `needs.ts` (needs, RFP, quotes, accept/reject/withdraw, engagements start/deliver/cancel/invoice, reviews, `/analytics`), `services.ts`, `partner_portal.ts` | UPGRADE | Five zones. **Leads** adds source provenance and need-to-capability scoring with the receipt behind each score, editable capability weights, and a recorded pass reason. **Proposals** adds the full lifecycle (drafts/sent/opened/decided) with win-and-loss reasons and open-count as a first-class signal. **Negotiations** is a new board where every card carries its one named blocker. **Retainers** adds renewal dates and **utilization against scope** (34% = churn risk, 118% = unbilled work). **Analytics** adds win rate, cycle time, forecast and loss patterns broken out by shape. Namespace warning: the canvas puts these at `/pipeline/{leads,proposals,…}`, which collides with the live investor deal pipeline at `/pipeline/{screening,commit,transactions}`. D395: `/partner/operations/*` retired; Proposals gained Withdraw on a submitted quote, and the engagement lifecycle it once shared a page with is on Delivery · Board. |

| Perks & Products | shared (founder + partner) | `/perks` (new) | `/perks` → `PerksPage` (Wave 4) | — (none) for perks/credits; adjacent `products.ts`, `catalog.ts`, `admin_promos.ts`, `billing.ts` | NEW | Credit-priced partner perk marketplace: claim modal with credit debit + redemption code, My Perks + credit ledger, partner submit with live preview + review queue, listing analytics. Distinct from `/marketplace` (services) and `/plans-and-pricing` (Stripe storefront). No perk or credit-ledger table exists. **Wave 4 shipped** it: migration 186 (`perks`, `perk_claims`, `perk_credit_ledger`, `perk_views`), `routes/perks.ts`, and `/perks` role-branched inside one route — founders browse and claim, partners submit and see their own listing stats, admins review. A perk is priced one of three ways: `credits` debits the ledger, `tier` is gated by the SAME `userMeetsTier` the rest of the product uses rather than a second notion of entitlement, and `money` quotes `price_cents` the partner invoices offline. Balance is `SUM(delta)` and is never stored; a claim and its debit go in one `DB.batch`, and two unique indexes (`perk_claims(perk_id, user_id)`, `perk_credit_ledger(user_id, kind, source_ref)`) make a double-spend unreachable rather than unlikely. **A second credit ledger, deliberately:** `intro_credit_ledger` (migration 150) is a different unit — one intro credit buys one warm introduction, where the canvas prices perks at 150–3,200 — so sharing the pool would impose an exchange rate nobody set; and its `bucket`/`kind` CHECK clauses cannot be extended without rebuilding a live table, which an additive migration must not do. **Not built, and why:** no monthly plan allowance. How many credits Growth or Studio includes is a commercial term nobody has set, and inventing one would put a made-up price in the product — the same reason Pricing is blocked in Wave 3. Credits enter only through an admin grant today, and the response carries `allowance_configured` so the page can say "no allowance is set up" instead of "not enough credits", which are very different things to tell someone with a zero balance. The catalogue also starts empty: the partners named in the canvas (Northlake, Sablefin, Oakbridge…) are placeholders, and listing them would invent commercial relationships. |

It splits `sharePlatforms` into three labelled parts: a 7-destination rail row (`shareQuick` — WhatsApp, Telegram, LinkedIn, Facebook, FB Stories, Instagram, IG Stories, the last three opening the modal because they need an asset), then the link, then **"Post and story formats"** (`shareMedia` — Instagram post 1:1, Instagram Stories 9:16, Facebook Stories 9:16, each with a drop slot, a caption and a download). It also adds Facebook to the modal's platform list with a real `facebook.com/sharer` URL plus a copied caption, and two new captions (`msgInstagram`, `msgStory` + code). **The three downloads are toast stubs in the canvas itself** — `download: () => this.toast('1:1 card downloaded …')` over a slot reading "Drop your 1:1 video or image here" — so building them means generating the card client-side or saying plainly that the asset is the referrer's to supply; it is not a wire to an endpoint. **Built 2026-09-07**, with the canvas's three "download" stubs replaced by real generation: `renderShareCard` draws the card on a `<canvas>` at 1080×1080 and 1080×1920 from the referrer's own code and QR, reusing the path the QR's Download PNG already used. **No drop slot was built**, deliberately — the canvas's slots had no store behind them and this platform has none either, so the vertical formats say the video is the referrer's to bring rather than accepting a file nothing can keep. Three live defects fixed in the same pass: the "Rewards earned" tile regex-scraped a `$` figure out of the free-text `reward_label` and otherwise fell back to a bare count, printing two units from one tile — it now reports the count and says "Rewards issued", because `reward_label` is `TEXT` by design and no amount column exists to total. `'5 days'` for "Avg. review time" was hard-coded; `avgReviewDaysForReferrer` now measures it from `referral_submissions.created_at` to the first `referral_submission_events` row carrying a verdict status, `GET /refer-earn/overview` returns `avg_review_days`, and null renders **"Not recorded"**. `rewardColor()`'s raw hexes are gone: `CHIP_TEXT` keys off the same `STATUS_TONE` tone as the pill, which closed a real disagreement — `converted`/`reward_eligible` rendered green as a chip and amber as text, `qualified`/`in_conversation` purple as a chip and amber as text, so one row was painted two ways. **Dark-mode variants were NOT added, and that is a correction rather than an omission**: they went in first, then came out when the render pass showed why. `referrals.css` hard-codes `.rf-card { background: #fff }`, holds zero dark rules, and the JSX carries ~200 literal light hexes — this page is light-only by construction, and a dark chip on a card that stays white in every theme is one element defecting from its surface. Converting the page is real work and its own change. `frontend/test/referrals_contract.test.mjs` now pins the status vocabulary across all three places that spell it out by hand — the page's `STATUS_TONE`, the worker's `STATUSES`/`STATUS_LABELS`, and migration 175's `CHECK` — plus `PIPELINE_FILTERS` and the verdict partition; it is the guard this page lacked entirely, modelled on `trust_center_contract.test.mjs` one page over. |

Nobody is stranded: `/kyc` still routes the same form and Settings still links it. Two honest reductions from the canvas — the document renders **Uploaded** / **Not recorded** because `document_uploaded` is a bare boolean, and **`id_number` is masked to the last four**, which the canvas is right about and `publicKycData` does not do. An absent value reads **"Not recorded"**, not the canvas's "Not provided": the platform cannot tell a value the user withheld from one it never asked for. The Entity tab **now draws the per-company card** (task #108, migration 220, D65). It stated the model instead for as long as the model made the card a lie — `corporate_profiles` upserts `ON CONFLICT(user_id)` and no `/trust/*` route took a company, so one user had one KYB and a selector over three companies would have changed nothing when clicked. `company_kyb_records` is a SECOND object beside the account's, never a replacement: D40 and D42 both say the account's entity and the company's must not drift into each other, and `corporate_profiles` is untouched. `GET|POST /api/trust/companies/kyb` take their company from the verified `X-Company-Id` header, never from the body. Also fixed: the pending-agreement label read `p.agreement_type`, a key the worker has never sent (`trust.ts:93` says the column does not exist), so every row fell through to the literal "Agreement" — it reads `document_type` now. **When it is built, keep the shipped `STATUS_TONE`.** The canvas's own `toneOf` enumerates only `satisfied/signed/verified/clear/none`, `in review/self-attested/pending/review` and `expired/rejected/block/blocked` — no `active`, `revoked`, `cancelled` or `waived`, which is precisely the hole PR #446 closed; applied literally it would render an in-force NDA and a revoked one the same grey. `waived` is load-bearing too: `computeTrustScore` counts it alongside satisfied, so the tone has to agree with the score. |

path with no worker handler, already recorded in `scripts/api-drift-baseline.json`
(58 `missing_route` entries), which is why CI stays green.

| Surface | Broken calls | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Network workspace (`/advisor/network/*`) | `/api/network-introductions/*`, `/api/organizations*` | The Network surface every role's sidebar links to. A second, working Network lives at `/network`. |
| Trust Center (`/trust`) | 9 routes: `/trust/kyb/status`, `/kyb/submit`, `/accreditation/*` (5), `/nda/status`, `/nda/:p/preview`, `/nda/sign` | The editable KYB / Accreditation / NDA cards. Trust Center v2 converts exactly these to read-only, so v2 is the fix, not merely a redesign. |

  is the empty-state on-ramp. See the Company Settings row above.
